# RGX-0078: PGEN regex parser SPEED — close the PCRE2-relative gap (RGX compile-time is way too slow vs PCRE2)

## Metadata

- Tree ID: `RGX-0078`
- Status: `queued` (created 2026-07-09 session #73; **precondition: regex is FEATURE-COMPLETE first** — director, this session). Not `active` until PCRE2 feature parity (`REGEX-PCRE2-FIDELITY`) + the RGX code-hook family (`REGEX-CODE-HOOKS`, incl. `(??{...})`) land.
- Roadmap lane: released-parser performance remediation (`regex`; downstream `rgx/pgen-issues/PGEN-RGX-0078.yaml`, `status:open`)
- Created: `2026-07-09`
- Owner: repo-local workflow
- Priority: **DIRECTOR-ELEVATED (2026-07-09, session #73): this is THE critical issue for the PGEN regex parser.** The feature set "is really, really good" — the blocker to RGX production use is SPEED. RGX showed (reproducible via `PGEN-RGX-0078.yaml`) that PGEN's regex parse-time (= RGX compile-time) is *way too slow* vs PCRE2. Correctness/features are NOT the issue; speed is. Sequenced AFTER feature-completeness, then it becomes the top regex priority.
- Prior durable record: [[project_rgx_0078_regex_slowness_followup]] (decision) + `docs/knowledge/rgx-0078-regex-slowness-followup.md` (KM card). This tree ACTIVATES that queued work.

## Goal

Close the **PCRE2-relative** speed gap for the PGEN `regex` parser so RGX's compile-time (which
delegates to PGEN's regex parse) is competitive with PCRE2. Measured baseline (RGX-0073 8-pattern
bench, geomean): PGEN parse is **~360× slower than PCRE2 no-JIT compile, ~85× slower than PCRE2 JIT**.
The absolute `<50µs` target was already met (release 1.1.30, round RGX-0073); the OPEN integration
criterion is the **ratio**.

## ⛔ HARD CONSTRAINT — non-negotiable (director, 2026-07-09, session #73)

**The speed effort SHALL NOT compromise feature accuracy or PCRE2 parity. Any speed optimization that
creates a correctness regression or breaks parity is REJECTED WITHOUT A SECOND THOUGHT — no deliberation,
no "it's only one edge case", no net-win trade-off.** Correctness/parity is a floor, not a variable to
trade against latency. Concretely, an optimization is admissible ONLY if, after it, EVERY correctness
oracle is still green at its pre-optimization value:
- `regex_pcre2_compile_oracle_gate` byte-identical (the PCRE2 corpus verdicts: `2189/1858/285/46` or
  better — never a single new false-accept/false-reject);
- regex cert-coverage `fully_certified` (`UNKNOWN=0`, `spf=0`) at seeds 0/7/42;
- `parse_harness_equivalence_gate` (regex byte-identical interpreter↔generated), `duality_hunt_gate`
  (no new signature), `regex_ast_shape_contract_gate`, and the released-parser pin tests — all green;
- RGX conformance unchanged.

If a candidate is faster but flips ANY verdict / breaks ANY gate, it does not land — full stop. Revert it
and find another lever. This is the operational form of [[feedback_correctness_before_speed]] for the
speed phase itself.

## Acceptance Criteria

- **Closure metric:** geomean(PGEN-parse / PCRE2-no-JIT-compile) **< 5×** over the PCRE2 test corpus
  (broader than the 8-pattern bench), measured via the vendorable `pgen_iteration_flow/` harness.
- **No correctness regression (HARD, per the constraint above):** the PCRE2 compile-oracle gate + RGX
  conformance stay green (the PCRE2 corpus is the oracle); regex cert-coverage stays `fully_certified`
  at seeds 0/7/42; equivalence/duality/ast_shape gates byte-identical. A regression = automatic reject.
- **Every change is profile-driven** (WHY+WHERE before fix) and keeps or improves the GLOBAL geomean
  ratio, measured before→after (`feedback_no_codebase_change_without_tool_backed_facts`).

## Methodology (grounded, tools-first)

- **Profile first.** macOS `sample` (or `cargo flamegraph`) on a hot regex parse to pin the actual
  bottleneck — act on the profiled fact, not a guess. Regex's cause is **DIFFERENT from SV's**: SV's
  was the per-rule `SemanticRuntimeState` clone in `with_semantic_runtime_rule_transaction`; regex has
  **no semantic predicates**, so it takes that function's FAST-PATH (skips the clone). So
  `PARSE-TERMINATION.3.1` does NOT help regex — the bottleneck is elsewhere (candidates to confirm by
  profile: packrat memo allocation per rule, `ParseNode`/clone churn, the `FxHashMap` memo, per-char
  overhead vs PCRE2's hyper-tuned bytecode compile + JIT).
- **Candidate techniques (evaluate AFTER profiling, keep iff the geomean improves):** memo/allocation
  tuning; arena / zero-copy `ParseNode`; reducing per-rule overhead; the cut operator / bounded memo
  (Mizushima 2010, PARSE-SOTA B2); avoiding redundant work. **Honest architectural caveat (my take,
  session #73):** PGEN's generated recursive-descent + packrat is inherently heavier than PCRE2's
  hand-tuned NFA/bytecode compiler + JIT. Reaching `<5×` may need STRUCTURAL work (arena allocation,
  a leaner memo strategy, possibly a regex-specific compiled fast path), not just micro-tuning — treat
  this as a research effort with a citation + worked mapping, not trial-and-revert.
- **Resources (in the RGX issue):** the vendorable `pgen_iteration_flow/` harness (standalone C PCRE2
  baselines + a self-contained Rust microbench + a one-shot driver, so PGEN runs the same
  PCRE2-relative measurement without RGX — see `pgen_iteration_flow/README.md`); the PCRE2 test corpus
  (correctness regression + a broader perf corpus).

## Cross-repo

The issue lives in the `rgx` repo (`rgx/pgen-issues/PGEN-RGX-0078.yaml`); the WORK is on PGEN's regex
parser (`grammars/regex.ebnf` + the shared engine/codegen — a parser-AGNOSTIC change where possible).
Coordinate the closure report back to the RGX issue.

  (`REGEX-CODE-HOOKS`, incl. `(??{...})`). Do NOT activate `RGX-0078` before feature-completeness.

## Session log

### Session #90 (2026-07-11) — ACTIVATION + `.1` SCOPE / `.2` PROFILE (profile-first, no code change yet)

**Prior-art inventory (so we do NOT re-tread).** The RGX-0073 campaign already landed **Optim #1–#16**
(rule-names-as-`&'static str`; cheap `Backtrack`; cached `logger.is_enabled()`; `FxHashMap` memo +
pre-size; borrow-`Regex`-in-place; opt-in mimalloc; fast-path/codegen-elided semantic-runtime wrapper
for predicate-free grammars; redundant-outer-clone elision; per-rule observability/recursion-guard +
memo elision for non-recursive rules). RGX-0078 then landed Optim #14/#15 (cached worker thread, inline
fast path for shallow inputs). `PARSE-TERMINATION.6.0` split the packrat memo by outcome (~20% faster).
Those were largely **allocation / memo / wrapper micro-tuning**. The 2026-07-11 reframing adds an
untested **structural** hypothesis-set (`longest_match` tournament evaluates EVERY branch; per-tournament
`semantic_runtime_state.checkpoint()` + `evaluation_order` heap alloc + string-keyed
`effective_deterministic_partition_*` lookups even for predicate-free regex) — to CONFIRM/REFUTE by
profile, then attack the systemic lever.

**Code-structure read (generated/regex_parser.rs, tournament region ~L3025–3060), NOT yet a profiled
cost:** each choice entry emits, before looping over branches: `semantic_runtime_state.checkpoint()`;
`effective_deterministic_partition_enabled(false)` + `effective_deterministic_partition_group(<str>,<str>)`
(string-keyed runtime lookups); `let mut evaluation_order: Vec<usize> = (0..N).collect()` (a heap
allocation per tournament); then per branch a `try_parse` closure. These are candidate systemic costs —
the profiler decides which dominates.

**Plan (tools-first, one measurement at a time):**
1. `.1` — rebuild `--release` perf binaries (STALE: `regex_perf_probe` Jul 4 vs regen'd parser Jul 11);
   run `regex_perf_probe` → current 8-pattern baseline (ns/pattern); this is the deterministic
   before-number. (PCRE2-relative geomean via the not-yet-vendored `pgen_iteration_flow` harness is the
   `.4` closure metric; the profile does not need it.)
2. `.2` — `PGEN_REPORT_MEMO_STATS=1` + macOS `sample` on a hot pattern (e.g. `character_class`,
   `anchor_complex`) → name the dominant self-time function. Then SYSTEMIC test: repeat the profile on a
   json + a vhdl parse, confirm/refute the cost lives in SHARED engine code (not a regex-only path).
3. Record WHERE+WHY here before any `.3` change. ⛔ EXCLUDE the pathological corpus cells (catastrophic
   `\(…\)` line 878/881 + 80-deep-paren line 1340) — the 8-pattern bench does not include them.

### PROFILE RESULTS (session #90) — tool-backed WHY + WHERE

**Method:** `sample <pid> 12 -mayDie` (macOS profiler, 1 ms) on release binaries: regex via
`regex_perf_probe` hot loop; json via a single `parseability_probe --parse json big.json` (740 KB,
4000-object array); vhdl via `--parse vhdl big.vhd` (146 KB, 1500 entities). Raw sample dumps +
`profile_all.sh` in the session scratchpad.

**REGEX self-time (9630 leaf samples, categorized):**
- **malloc / free / memops — 5719 (59.4%)** ← the dominant SYMPTOM (`_xzm_free` 1729, `_xzm_xzone_malloc`
  536, `_malloc_zone_malloc` 491, `_free` 365, `memmove` 449, `memset`/`__bzero` 438, `___rdl_alloc` 209…).
- semantic-runtime (rollback + per-rule annotation lookups) — 668 (6.9%)
- SipHash / hashmap — 576 (6.0%) (`sip::Hasher::write` 321 + `hash_one` 103 + `reserve_rehash` 90 + insert 48)
- string formatting — 419 (4.4%) (`format_inner`/`fmt::write`/`String::write_str`/`Formatter::pad`/`Display::fmt`)
- clone / drop — 144 (1.5%)

**Call-graph attribution of the 59% malloc (subtree sample sums):**
- **`SemanticRuntimeState::rollback_to_named` = 2285 samples ≈ 24% of total** ← the SINGLE largest
  allocation source. Called on every failed speculation (backtrack). Its children show
  `Vec::spec_from_iter_nested::from_iter` + `_malloc_zone_malloc` + `__bzero` = the **`self.scopes =
  active_chain.iter().map(..).collect()` rebuild (`semantic_runtime.rs:2804`)** and the
  `active_chain_snapshot.clone()` (`:2793`), both run UNCONDITIONALLY per rollback.
- `HashMap::insert` (181+45+12+5) + `reserve_rehash` (133+21+4) — memo + annotation-table churn.
- `ParseContent::to_json_value` 69, `drop_in_place<ParseContent>` 64, `extract_delta_since` 60,
  `push_rule_context` 33.

**SYSTEMIC — CONFIRMED across grammars (shared engine code, not a regex path):**
- **json** (predicate-free): `create_contextual_error` 174 (the backtrack-path error-construction analog
  of regex's rollback/format strings), `RecursionGuard::check_cycle` 130 (shared; also in regex 43),
  the same malloc family, `sip::Hasher::write` 18, `BTreeMap`/`ParseNode`/`String` clones.
- **vhdl** (predicate-CARRYING; small sample — parse finished fast): top frames still `_xzm_free`/`_free`/
  `memmove`/`malloc` + `sip::Hasher::write` 6 + `String`/`BTreeMap` clones — same shape.

**REFUTED (profile beats inference — [[project_uvm_memory_not_the_memo]]):** the "per-parser construction
rebuilds the annotation table ⇒ ~160µs floor" hypothesis. Call graph: `RegexParser::new` ≈ **~70 samples
(~0.7%)** vs `parse_full` **9584**. Construction is NEGLIGIBLE; the PARSE dominates. The 163µs floor for
`"test"` is the per-character `atom`-tournament (each char tries ~30 atom alternatives under
`longest_match`, most backtrack — each failed speculation pays the rollback allocation above). This
CONFIRMS the reframing's hypothesis #1 (every-branch tournament) as the DRIVER and #2 (per-speculation
overhead) as the COST, and correctly kills the construction red herring.

**Ranked systemic levers (profile-indicated, for `.3`+):**
1. **Backtrack/rollback allocation (~24%)** — `.3` gates the unconditional `active_chain` clone + `scopes`
   `.collect()` in `rollback_to_named` on scope-state-changed. No regen; correctness-neutral.
2. **Per-rule SipHash annotation-table lookups** (`needs_raw_post_capture_for_rule`/`_final_`/
   `pre_predicates_for_rule`/`has_rule` ≈ 330 + hashing 560): every rule entry SipHash-looks-up a
   mostly-empty table for predicate-free grammars. Candidate: FxHash the table (Optim #6 precedent) and/or
   codegen-elide the lookups for annotation-free rules.
3. **Reduce per-speculation allocation churn** (ParseContent/Vec per branch attempt) — the longest_match
   tournament trying every atom alternative per char is the structural driver; first-set predictive
   dispatch is the deeper (higher-risk, later) lever.
4. **Backtrack-path trace strings** — the generated `try_parse` Err arm builds `entry.0.to_string()` +
   `format!("{} (try_parse Err)")` per failed speculation for a `rollback_to_named` context that is only
   consumed when facts/scopes were actually rolled back OR trace is on. Codegen change (regen) — sequenced
   after the no-regen wins.

### Speed technique space (director brainstorm, 2026-07-11 — "JIT/AOT? think out-of-the-box, be creative")

The full menu of speed techniques mapped onto PGEN's actual problem (ONE-SHOT parse of a pattern that
PRODUCES A TYPED AST — = RGX's compile-time). Ranked by leverage × confidence × effort. Correctness floor
NEVER traded ([[feedback_correctness_before_speed]] ⛔) for any of these.

**Framing correction (the useful surprise):**
- **AOT — we ALREADY have it.** Codegen compiles `grammars/*.ebnf → generated/*_parser.rs → rustc/LLVM →
  native machine code`. The generated parser IS AOT-compiled native code (the interpreter is only a debug
  path). So AOT is the FOUNDATION, not a future lever.
- **JIT — does NOT fit the parse phase.** PCRE2's JIT specializes the MATCHER for a pattern and amortizes
  the compile cost over MANY match calls. Parsing a pattern is ONE-SHOT — nothing to amortize (a JIT compile
  would cost more than the single parse it speeds). JIT-the-grammar is redundant with AOT. So "add a JIT"
  is a category mismatch for RGX-0078. (JIT WOULD help RGX's downstream MATCHING phase — but that's RGX's
  job, not PGEN's parse.)

**A) Free AOT wins we are LEAVING ON THE TABLE (cheap, high-confidence, parser-agnostic, correctness-neutral).**
The release build uses cargo DEFAULTS — MEASURED: no `[profile.release]` in `rust/Cargo.toml`, no
`.cargo/config` target-cpu, no PGO. So `lto=false`, `codegen-units=16`, no `target-cpu`. Candidates:
1. **`lto = "fat"` + `codegen-units = 1`** — classic 5-20% on compute-heavy native code, zero code change.
2. **`target-cpu=native`** (or a modern baseline) — SIMD/instruction-set wins for terminal scanning.
3. **PGO (profile-guided optimization)** — ⭐ the AOT-side ANALOG of JIT's benefit: train on the PCRE2 corpus,
   let LLVM re-lay-out hot/cold branches + inlining from the real profile. ~10-20% on branchy parser code.
   This is the honest answer to "can we JIT?" — PGO gives ~half the JIT win (specialized native code from a
   real profile) at AOT time, no runtime cost. Underused, high-value.
   (Caveat: verify determinism + the release binary is what RGX ships; PGO needs a repeatable training run.)

**B) Out-of-the-box — attack the problem, not the inner loop.**
4. ⭐ **Eliminate the parse, don't just speed it (highest real-workload leverage).** RGX compiles MANY
   patterns, MANY of them repeats (across runs / common shapes `\d+` `\w+` `[a-z]+`). A **persistent parse
   cache** (hash pattern string → cached AST, clone on hit) makes every repeat compile ~free — a hashmap
   lookup beats ANY parser. "Don't parse at all" beats every inner-loop tuning. Attacks RGX's ACTUAL cost
   (fixed per-pattern overhead × repetition) more than asymptotics. (An RGX-side cache, or a PGEN embedding-API
   cache.)
5. **Specialized regex fast-path parser.** Regex is THE hot grammar with shallow structure; generate (or
   hand-write) a leaner regex-specific parser that skips the general packrat/semantic-runtime machinery.
   Trades generality for speed on the one grammar that needs it (the "don't use the general engine for the
   hot case" bet). Bigger; correctness proven against the general parser as oracle.
6. **DFA/table-driven token layer.** For the lexical layer (character classes, literal runs), compile a DFA
   like a lexer generator instead of recursive-descent per-char matching. Structural; the algorithmic analog
   of what makes hand-tuned matchers fast.

**C) Structural engine levers (the profile-indicated core work).**
7. **Arena / bump allocation for ParseNode** — attacks the 59% malloc directly (simdjson-style: one arena,
   mass-free). The single biggest allocation lever; parser-agnostic; larger change.
8. **First-set predictive dispatch** — don't try every longest-match branch; skip alternatives that can't
   match the next char. Attacks the backtracking MULTIPLIER, not the per-attempt cost. Deepest algorithmic win.
9. **Per-rule annotation-lookup elision / FxHash** — the ~6% SipHash per-rule lookups (`.3`-adjacent).
10. **SIMD terminal scanning** — character-class / literal runs via SIMD (hyperscan/simdjson-style). Narrow.
11. **Interning + zero-copy** — `&str` slices into the input instead of `String` copies; extend the
    `&'static` rule-name interning (Optim #3) to more transient allocations.

**Sequencing note:** the profile-indicated engine wins (`.3`, then 9 → 7 → 8) come first because they are
measured and parser-agnostic. The build-flag wins (A) are a cheap parallel slice (own leaf) worth grabbing
early since they are ~free. The out-of-the-box bets (4, 5) are the highest real-workload leverage and worth
a design spike — especially the parse cache (4), which may be the single most impactful thing for RGX's
"many patterns" reality. Captured in the book *latency campaign* chapter.

### `.3` (session #90/#91) — gate the rollback scope-restoration on scope-state-changed

**The change (one function, shared engine, no codegen/regen):** in
`SemanticRuntimeState::rollback_to_named` (`rust/src/ast_pipeline/semantic_runtime.rs`), wrap the arena
truncate + `active_chain = snapshot.clone()` + closed-flag reset + `self.scopes = …collect()` rebuild
(the ~L2788–2814 block) in `if self.scope_arena.len() > scope_arena_len || self.active_chain !=
checkpoint.active_chain_snapshot { … }`. The fact-index removal + `facts.truncate` +
`deferred_obligations.truncate` stay unconditional (already no-ops when nothing changed). The trailing
`debug_assert_eq!(self.scopes.len(), checkpoint.scope_len)` stays outside the guard (holds either way:
`scopes.len() == active_chain.len() == snapshot.len() == scope_len` when the chain is unchanged).

#### Acceptance Checklist (enforced)
- [x] **REPRODUCE / ISSUE** — `.2` profile: `SemanticRuntimeState::rollback_to_named` = ~24% of regex parse
  self-time (2285/9630 call-graph samples), children `Vec::from_iter`+`malloc` = the unconditional
  `active_chain.clone()`:2793 + `scopes.collect()`:2804 on every backtrack.
- [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — the scope-state restoration in `rollback_to_named`
  (`semantic_runtime.rs:2793` + `:2804`) runs UNCONDITIONALLY on every failed speculation, allocating a
  Vec clone + a Vec rebuild even when the speculation had zero net effect on scope state (the common case
  under the `longest_match` atom-tournament). Confirmed by `sample` call-graph attribution (`.2`).
- [x] **FIX** — engine (`semantic_runtime.rs:2800`), tier: shared parser-runtime primitive (parser-agnostic,
  benefits all parsers + the interpreter, which share this method). Gate on scope-state-changed
  (`scope_state_changed = self.scope_arena.len() > scope_arena_len || self.active_chain !=
  checkpoint.active_chain_snapshot`). SOUND: `scopes` is lockstep with `active_chain`
  (`open_scope`:2940-2941 push both / `close_scope`:2964-2965 pop both) ⇒ chain-unchanged + arena-not-grown
  ⇒ `scopes` already correct ⇒ skip is a no-op; `debug_assert_eq!(scopes.len(), scope_len)` stays OUTSIDE
  the guard and holds either way.
- [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — `regex_perf_probe` geomean of noise-floor MINS (immune to the background-load
  contention that inflated means/p99), **decisive stash baseline** (`git stash` ONLY `semantic_runtime.rs`,
  rebuild, re-measure): **no-fix 422µs → with-fix 344µs = −18.6%** on the 8-pattern bench. (The `.3` checklist's
  earlier "before = 496µs" was a contention-inflated reading; the noise-floor mins are the honest figure.)
- [x] **NO REGRESSION** — ⛔ HARD constraint SATISFIED, every oracle green at its pre-optimization value,

### `.4` (session #91/#92) — FREE AOT build-flag wins

**Framing (from the `.2` "Speed technique space" block, section A):** the release build currently uses cargo
DEFAULTS — MEASURED: no `[profile.release]` in `rust/Cargo.toml`, no `.cargo/config` (both confirmed this
session). So `lto=false`, `codegen-units=16`, no `target-cpu`, no PGO. These are cheap, high-confidence,
parser-agnostic, correctness-neutral wins we are leaving on the table. A build-profile change cannot alter
program semantics (barring latent UB, which would itself be a bug to surface) — so the correctness floor is
even more robust here than for `.3`; the HARD CONSTRAINT is still proven each sub-slice by re-running the
full oracle battery byte-identical.

**Measurement discipline (inherited from `.3`):** the honest metric is the geomean of the `regex_perf_probe`
**noise-floor MINS** (immune to background-load contention — e.g. a concurrent NEXSIM run — that inflates
means/p99), at `--samples 2000 --warmup 200` (the `.1` pinned methodology). Decisive pairing: the SOURCE is
byte-identical between baseline and treatment (only `[profile.release]` differs), so both are full release
rebuilds differing only in the profile — a clean single-variable experiment.

#### `.4.a` — `lto = "fat"` + `codegen-units = 1`

The unambiguous, reproducible, machine-INDEPENDENT first sub-slice (unlike `.4.b`/`.4.c`, which are
machine-specific). Classic 5–20% on compute-heavy native code, zero source change.

#### `.4.b` — `target-cpu=native` — REJECTED (measured REGRESSION, not a win)

**Hypothesis (to test, not assume):** `-C target-cpu=native` lets LLVM use the host CPU's full instruction
set (SIMD, newer scalar ops) for the terminal-scanning / character-class inner loops — the commonly-assumed
"free" win. **Discipline: MEASURE, don't infer** ([[project_uvm_memory_not_the_memo]]).

**Method (decisive, drift-controlled):** built `regex_perf_probe` with `RUSTFLAGS="-C target-cpu=native"` on
top of the `.4.a` fat-LTO profile (10m10s), saved the binary (distinct sha256 from the fat-LTO-only binary),
then measured it ALTERNATELY back-to-back against the saved fat-LTO-only binary (`regex_perf_probe` 2000/200,
geomean of noise-floor mins). `RUSTFLAGS` lived only in the build command's env — NOT written to any tracked
file, so the committed/shipped config is unchanged (fat-LTO only, no `.cargo/config`).

**Result — `target-cpu=native` is ~+4% SLOWER, uniformly:** fat-LTO-only `313,008 / 313,163 / 313,303 ns`
vs +native `327,219 / 327,822 / 324,212 ns` = **+3.5% to +4.6% REGRESSION** (three alternated readings, zero
overlap). Per-pattern: 7 of 8 patterns ~+3.6…+4.6% slower (digit_sequence +4.6%, character_class +3.8%,
alternation +4.2%, capture_groups +3.6%, url_simple +4.0%, email_basic +4.2%, anchor_complex +3.8%); only
`literal_simple` is flat (−0.5%). A SYSTEMATIC regression, not an outlier.

**Why (likely):** PGEN's parser is branchy, control-flow-bound, small-input (the `.2` profile: 59% malloc +
per-char `longest_match` tournament — nothing data-parallel to vectorize). `target-cpu=native` codegen
(aggressive autovectorization + apple-m4-specific instruction selection) bloats the code with SIMD setup that
never pays off on tiny inputs, hurting I-cache locality → a net loss. `target-cpu=native` is NOT a universal
win; it can regress branch-heavy code — exactly this case.

**DECISION (autonomous — the data decides; two independent reasons both say no):** do NOT bake
`target-cpu=native`. (1) It is a MEASURED regression here (~+4%); (2) it is machine-specific (a `native`
binary is not portable across CPUs, so it could not be baked into a shipped config anyway). No bake-vs-consumer
decision to surface — there is nothing worth shipping. **Consumer-guidance note:** downstream consumers (RGX)
should NOT assume `target-cpu=<host>` helps — it regressed the PGEN parser ~4% on Apple Silicon; measure the
target workload before adopting it. NO code change; NO correctness battery needed (nothing landed). Frontier →
`.4.c` PGO.

#### `.4.c` — PGO (profile-guided optimization) — MEASURED MODEST (~−1% lower bound), NOT LANDED

**Hypothesis (director-steered):** PGO is the AOT analog of PCRE2's JIT benefit — train on the bench corpus,
let LLVM re-lay-out hot/cold branches + inline from the real profile (~10-20% on branchy code potential).
Tooling feasible: `llvm-profdata` present in the rustup llvm-tools sysroot; rustc `-Cprofile-generate/-use`.

**Method (ceiling probe).** Single-binary PGO on `regex_perf_probe` (train+measure on the 8-pattern bench =
an OPTIMISTIC upper bound). (1) Instrument build — initially with fat-LTO, but `-Cprofile-generate` + `lto=fat`
is PATHOLOGICALLY slow (killed at 23 min, still going); redone WITHOUT fat-LTO (`CARGO_PROFILE_RELEASE_LTO=false
CARGO_PROFILE_RELEASE_CODEGEN_UNITS=16`, 16m53s) — the correct methodology (the instrument build only records
branch weights; LTO belongs on the optimized build). (2) Train: ran the instrumented binary → 16 `.profraw`.
(3) `llvm-profdata merge` → 20 MB `merged.profdata`. (4) Optimized build: fat-LTO + cu=1 (the shipped config)
+ `-Cprofile-use` (10m30s). (5) Alternate back-to-back vs the saved fat-LTO-only binary.

**Result: fat-LTO `309,369 / 307,841 ns` vs fat-LTO+PGO `305,745 / 305,151 ns` = ~−1.0%** (PGO consistently
faster both rounds, distinct sha256). **⚠️ LOWER BOUND — 10,322 `no profile data available for function`
warnings:** the profile came from a non-LTO/cu=16 instrument build, and the fat-LTO/cu=1 optimized build inlines
functions differently, so most functions' CFG hashes did not match and PGO was only partially applied. The hot
leaf functions (called often, less likely to be inlined away) most likely DID match, so ~−1% is a reasonable —
if conservative — estimate. A fully-clean number needs the pathologically-slow (~30 min) fat-LTO instrument.

**DECISION (autonomous — robust to the exact number): do NOT land PGO.** Two independent reasons:
1. **Bounded ceiling.** The `.2` profile pins the dominant cost at **59% malloc**; PGO improves branch
   layout/inlining but CANNOT reduce allocation volume — so it structurally cannot be a large win here.
   (Confirmed: even the optimistic overfit ceiling measured only ~−1%.)
2. **Shipping burden not justified.** Landing PGO means baking an instrument→train→merge→rebuild PIPELINE into
   how RGX builds PGEN (a representative training corpus + a reproducible profile) — significant CI/build-process
   complexity that a ~1-few% gain does not justify, especially vs the `.5` engine levers, which attack the
   actual bottleneck (the malloc) with simpler parser-agnostic SOURCE changes and no build-process change.

NO code change; NO correctness battery needed (nothing landed). The exact clean number is NOT decision-relevant
(the shipping-burden + bounded-ceiling arguments hold regardless), so the ~30 min fat-LTO instrument is skipped.

**Build-flag phase (`.4`) COMPLETE:** `.4.a` LTO+cu=1 LANDED **−6.7%** (shipped) · `.4.b` target-cpu=native
REJECTED (~+4% regression) · `.4.c` PGO measured-modest (~−1% lower bound), not landed. The cheap/safe build-flag
wins are banked; the real remaining speed is the `.5` SOURCE/ENGINE levers attacking the profiled 59% malloc.

### `.5` (session #93, 2026-07-12) — SOURCE/ENGINE levers

The profile-indicated real remaining speed. Each `.5` sub-slice is a fresh, dedicated slice that MUST pass the
full `.3`/`.4.a` oracle battery under the ⛔ HARD CONSTRAINT (any correctness/parity regression = REJECT WITHOUT
SECOND THOUGHT). Sequenced lowest-risk-first so the (expensive, ~12-min fat-LTO) rebuild+measure cycle banks
confidence before the risky structural work: `.5.a` FxHash annotation lookups (this slice) → then per-speculation
arena/reuse (the 59% malloc) → first-set predictive dispatch → parse cache.

#### `.5.a` — FxHash the two per-rule-entry annotation-table lookup maps

**The change (one function-group in one file, shared engine, NO codegen/regen):** in
`CompiledSemanticRuntimeAnnotations` (`rust/src/ast_pipeline/semantic_runtime.rs`), switch the two per-rule-entry
lookup maps `directives_by_rule` + `branch_directives_by_rule` from the std `HashMap` (SipHash) to
`rustc_hash::FxHashMap` (the Optim-#6 memo hasher). The public constructors (`from_parts`, `from_rule_directives`)
keep taking `std::HashMap` and convert at the boundary (`.into_iter().collect()`, one-time O(rules) at construction);
the `compile_semantic_runtime_annotations` path builds `FxHashMap` directly. So the generated parser's emitted
`from_parts(...)` / `set_fact_kinds(...)` calls (all pass `std::collections::HashMap`) are UNCHANGED — no
codegen/regen. `fact_kinds` / `predicate_defs` are deliberately left std (not the profile-named hot path; touching
them would ripple through `set_fact_kinds` / `clone_predicate_defs` / the `fact_kinds()` iterator API).

##### Acceptance Checklist (enforced)
- [x] **REPRODUCE / ISSUE** — `.2` profile (macOS `sample`, release): the SipHash/hashmap bucket = **6.0%** of
  regex parse self-time (`sip::Hasher::write` 321 + `hash_one` 103 + `reserve_rehash` 90 + insert 48); the
  call-graph named the per-rule-entry lookup functions `has_rule` / `pre_predicates_for_rule` /
  `needs_raw_post_capture_for_rule` / `needs_raw_final_capture_for_rule` (~330 lookup samples), ALL of which
  funnel through `directives_by_rule.get(rule_name)` / `.contains_key(rule_name)`, evaluated on every rule entry
  and every backtrack under the `longest_match` atom-tournament.
- [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — `CompiledSemanticRuntimeAnnotations.directives_by_rule` +
  `.branch_directives_by_rule` (`semantic_runtime.rs:1174-1175`) were `std::collections::HashMap`, i.e. the std
  default `RandomState` **SipHash** hasher. SipHash is a cryptographic hash (DoS-resistant, slow); for the tiny
  static string keys of these tables, hashing dominates the lookup. Every rule entry SipHash-hashes the rule
  name to probe a table that MISSES for most (predicate-free) rules — pure overhead. WHERE: the two field decls
  + their two constructors + the compile-fn locals, all in `semantic_runtime.rs`.
- [x] **FIX** — engine (`semantic_runtime.rs`), tier: shared parser-runtime primitive (parser-AGNOSTIC — every
  grammar's generated parser AND the interpreter share `CompiledSemanticRuntimeAnnotations`). Swap the two maps'
  hasher to `FxHashMap`; keep public signatures on `std::HashMap` + convert at the boundary ⇒ NO codegen/regen.
  BYTE-IDENTICAL BY CONSTRUCTION: the two maps are accessed EXCLUSIVELY by point lookup (`get`/`contains_key`) and
  are only ever *counted* (`.keys()` in `len()`, order-independent) — never iterated for any output-affecting
  purpose — so a hasher swap cannot change parse output. FxHash's fixed seed is also strictly MORE deterministic
  than the std `RandomState` (which already randomizes iteration order per process, yet the oracles are
  deterministic at seeds 0/7/42 — independent proof nothing depends on these maps' order).
- [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — **DECISIVE drift-controlled before→after** (built BOTH release binaries fat-LTO,
  distinct sha256 `ff61ca80…` no-fix vs `674c3ea4…` with-fix, measured ALTERNATELY back-to-back, `regex_perf_probe

  ≈303µs). CLEAN SEPARATION — worst with-fix round (306,160) < best no-fix round (317,913), zero overlap; every

  - **regex cert-coverage** (debug `ast_pipeline`, seeds 0/7/42): `total=267 proof=9 witness=258 UNKNOWN=0

### `.5.b`/`.5.c` (session #94, 2026-07-12) — the string lever is a SYMPTOM; the root cause is "try every branch"

**Trigger (tool-backed correction of the `.5` re-profile WHERE).** Beginning `.5.b` (defer the try_parse Err arm
`caller_context` Strings), a tools-first audit of ALL `rollback_to_named` codegen emission sites
(`grep -n rollback_to_named ast_based_generator.rs`) + a grep of the CURRENT committed regex parser found:
- `ast_based_generator.rs:6788-6801` (the try_parse Err arm the re-profile named) → **2 sites** in `generated/regex_parser.rs`.
- `ast_based_generator.rs:2333` (`rollback_to_named(cp, Some(rule_name))`) → passes a `&'static str`, **no allocation**.
- `ast_based_generator.rs:3873` (the **C3-B per-branch tournament cleanup** `format!("{} (C3-B branch {}/{} cleanup)")`,
  emitted UNCONDITIONALLY per branch of every multi-branch tournament) → **993 sites**.

The `.5` re-profile call-graph (`reprofile.txt`) resolves it: `RegexParser::parse_atom` = **7565 samples** (the
dominant leaf), with `alloc::fmt::format_inner` / `String::write_str` / `SemanticRuntimeState::rollback_to_named`
ALL appearing as its nested children. So the profiled ≈6% string-formatting bucket is dominated by the **993-site
C3-B cleanup inside the `longest_match` atom-tournament**, NOT the 2 try_parse arms — the `.5.b` fix as scoped is
~null for regex. The `.5.b` edit was implemented (byte-identical by construction — the `caller_context` feeds only
the `pgen_trace_high!` trace label) and then **REVERTED** (a near-null measurement is not a lever worth the
~12-min×N fat-LTO oracle-battery cost, and the string cost is a symptom — see below). Repo returned clean.

**Root cause (director, session #94): "we let the parser blindly try every branch in the hope one succeeds — naive;
works for accuracy, not speed."** The 59% malloc AND the 6% string-formatting are the SAME defect seen twice: the
`longest_match` tournament evaluates EVERY branch at each position (regex `atom` ≈ 30 alternatives per char), and
each losing branch pays `try_parse` + `extract_delta_since` + `format!` (the C3-B label) + `rollback_to_named` +
its allocations. Attacking the string (or the malloc, or the hashing) treats symptoms; attacking "evaluate every
branch" removes the losing branches' cost at the source.

### `.5.c` DESIGN — first-set predictive dispatch (parser/EBNF-agnostic, SOUND under longest_match)

**The idea (director, session #94).** On a multi-branch / Or rule, look one char ahead and only evaluate the
branches that could actually begin a match there; skip the rest. "Select the matching branch and commit" — with the
critical soundness refinement below.

**Prior art (research-grounded, [[feedback_research_grounded_sota_no_trial_and_revert]]).** Classic predictive
parsing: LL(1)/LL(k) FIRST / director sets (Dragon book) choose the production without backtracking; ANTLR's
adaptive **ALL(\*)** (Parr & Fisher) builds lookahead DFAs to predict the alternative; re2c/flex dispatch on the
first char via a DFA; PEG "prefix"/first-char guards (Ford). We adopt the **prune** form (FIRST-set director-set),
NOT the full ALL(\*) commit machinery — because our `|` is a longest-match TOURNAMENT, pruning preserves semantics
with none of ALL(\*)'s unbounded-lookahead complexity.

**Soundness contract (the ⛔ HARD CONSTRAINT, byte-identical proof).**
- **PRUNE, don't commit.** For each branch compute `first_set` = a SOUND OVER-APPROXIMATION of the input bytes that
  can begin a match (superset — when uncertain, `ANY`). Before evaluating a NON-nullable branch, peek `c = input[pos]`
  (or EOF); if `c ∉ branch.first_set`, SKIP it. A skipped branch would have failed at char 1 in today's code →
  contributes nothing to the tournament → identical winner. Soundness rests ENTIRELY on `first_set` being a
  superset (never prune a branch that could match).
- **Nullable branches are NEVER pruned** (they can match empty / zero-length regardless of the next char).
- **"Commit" is sound ONLY for a singleton survivor set.** When the FIRST-set filter leaves ≥2 candidate branches,
  the tournament STILL runs among the survivors (longest_match depends on FULL match length, not the first char:
  `atom := "a" | "a" "b"` on `"ab"` — both survive 'a', only the tournament picks `"ab"`). Committing to one there
  = the retired `EarlierAlwaysMatches` unsoundness ([[project_earlier_always_matches_unsound_backtracking]]). For a
  terminal-disjoint regex `atom` the filter collapses ~30 → 1 in the COMMON case, so the "commit" behavior emerges
  automatically and soundly; the multi-survivor case degrades gracefully to a smaller tournament.
- **Conservative (`ANY`, never prune) for:** `.`/any-char, lookahead `&`/`!`, anchors `^`/`$`, `@predicate`/zero-width
  assertions, unresolved refs, case-insensitive/unicode classes not yet modeled, and ANY construct whose first byte
  is not statically known. This keeps soundness absolute at the cost of not pruning those (still correct, just no win
  there). Applies under EVERY `@branch_policy` (a can't-match branch is dead under ordered / priority_first /
  longest_match alike).

**Shape (parser/EBNF-agnostic; NO grammar-name gating; ALL parsers inherit it — [[feedback_features_parser_agnostic_enable_all_parsers]]).**
1. ANALYSIS: a grammar-agnostic FIRST-set pass over the normalized gen-AST (the `--dump-gen-ast` IR) — literal →
   {first char}; char-class → its set; ref → FIRST(ref) transitively to a fixpoint (recursion-safe); sequence →
   FIRST(head) ∪ (FIRST(tail) if head nullable); quantifier `*`/`?` → nullable; everything uncertain → `ANY`.
   Emit a compact per-branch first-set descriptor (byte bitset / small-set) + a `nullable` flag.
2. CODEGEN: for each multi-branch tournament, before a branch's try, emit `if branch_can_start(next_byte) { …existing
   branch eval… }` (skip otherwise). `ANY`/nullable branches emit no guard (always tried). No change to the
   tournament selection logic → longest_match winner identical.
3. This is a CODEGEN change ⇒ regen ALL parsers + verify regen actually happened
   ([[feedback_verify_sv_parser_regen_mtime]] — assert parser-mtime > grammar-mtime / a real behavior delta).

**Acceptance (this leaf):** decisive drift-controlled `regex_perf_probe` geomean before→after (build BOTH release
binaries fat-LTO, measure alternately) showing a real improvement; AND the FULL oracle battery byte-identical at its
pre-optimization value under the ⛔ HARD CONSTRAINT — PCRE2 compile-oracle fix-vs-nofix diff EMPTY (`1878/310/262/48`,
safe subset, 80-deep-paren excluded), regex cert `fully_certified` UNKNOWN=0 spf `0/1/1` seeds 0/7/42,
`parse_harness_equivalence_gate` (interp↔gen byte-identical, ALL grammars — this is the strongest cross-parser
soundness proof for a FIRST-set over-approximation bug), `parse_harness_combinator_gate` (the per-combinator choice
discrimination incl. `a|ab`), `parse_harness_semantic_gate` (36/36), `duality_hunt_gate` (no new signature),
`regex_ast_shape_contract_gate`, clippy. A FIRST-set that is too tight (prunes a matchable branch) shows up as a
divergence in equivalence/combinator/PCRE2 — the gates ARE the soundness oracle. Any flip = REJECT, retighten the
over-approximation to `ANY`.

**Children:**
- ID: `.5.c.1`  Status: `done` (session #94, 2026-07-12 — SCOUT + DESIGN SPIKE, docs-only, no code) — locate the
  multi-branch tournament codegen + the FIRST-set computation surface; confirm feasibility + soundness-alignment +
  regex prunability BEFORE any code. FINDINGS below. Next: `.5.c.2` (the codegen guard + regen + measure + battery).

#### `.5.c.1` SCOUT FINDINGS (session #94, tools-first — all de-risking the codegen implementation)

**F1 — reusable FIRST-set analysis EXISTS.** `annotation_validator.rs:101` `struct FirstSetSummary { terminals:
HashSet<String>, nullable: bool, unresolved: bool }` + `branch_first_set()` (`:2151`) / `atom_first_set()` (`:2267`)
/ `rule_first_set()` (`:2340`). It is a SOUND over-approximation already: Sequence (FIRST of leading elements until
a non-nullable one), Or (union), Quantified `?`/`*`/`{0,..}` → nullable, Lookahead → forced nullable, rule_reference
→ transitive with a `visiting_rules` CYCLE GUARD (returns `unresolved: true` on a cycle) + a `first_set_cache`, and a
`MAX_FIRST_SET_DEPTH=24` bound (→ `unresolved: true` past it). The conservative escape hatch is `unresolved: true`
(regex-token terminals, unknown token types, unresolved refs, depth/cycle cutoffs all set it). ⇒ the PRUNABLE
predicate for a branch is exactly `!summary.nullable && !summary.unresolved && next_char ∉ first_bytes(summary)`,
where `first_bytes` = `{ t.first char for t in terminals }` (terminals are stored quoted `'X..'` — take the first
char of the UNQUOTED value). Everything else is ALWAYS tried (never pruned) ⇒ soundness by construction.

**F2 — the codegen tournament operates on `ASTNode` (alignment SOLVED).** `generate_or_logic(alternatives:
&[ASTNode], rule_name, filename)` (`ast_based_generator.rs:3462`), branch loop `for idx in 0..branch_count { let
alternative = &alternatives[idx]; … }` (`:3614`), each branch an emitted match arm `#branch_index => { …checkpoint →
try → should_take → C3-B cleanup… }` (`:3646`). Because codegen iterates the SAME `ASTNode` type the FIRST-set
analysis consumes, the guard for branch `idx` can be computed from the EXACT `alternatives[idx]` node it guards →
aligned BY CONSTRUCTION (no raw-AST-vs-gen-AST mismatch). The generator threads `grammar_tree: &HashMap<String,
ASTNode>` everywhere (`:74`, `:352`, `:414`, `:3462`-adjacent), so transitive rule-ref resolution runs against the
SAME normalized (post-LR-elimination) tree codegen emits from — NOT the annotation_validator's separate pre-codegen
pass. ⇒ REUSE THE ALGORITHM, NOT THE CACHED RESULT: extract the FIRST-set fns into a shared module (or a codegen
helper) that `generate_or_logic` calls with its own `grammar_tree`.

**F3 — regex prunability is HIGH.** `grammars/regex.ebnf:365` `atom = literal | whitespace_literal | dot |
backreference | quoted_literal | escape | … | char_class | … | group` — ~24 rule-ref branches, most beginning with a
DISTINCT known metacharacter (`dot`→`.`, `char_class`→`[`, `group`/`lookaround`/`atomic_group`/…→`(`, `escape`/
`backreference`→`\`, anchors `^`/`$` live in `piece` not `atom`). Only the catch-all `literal`/`literal_char` (a
regex-token) is `unresolved` → correctly always-tried. So on a plain literal input char (e.g. `a`), ~24 atom
branches collapse to ~1-2 survivors — the exact "blindly tries every branch" waste the `.5` profile pinned
(`parse_atom` 7565 samples, malloc+`format!`+`rollback` its children). Large, real win; SOUND (pruned metacharacter
branches genuinely cannot match `a`).

**F4 — guard placement (byte-identical).** A pruned branch = a branch that would fail at char 1 producing nothing.
So the guard must SKIP the branch's ENTIRE body (checkpoint → try → extract_delta → C3-B `format!` → rollback →
should_take): emit, at the top of the branch arm, `if <branch prunable at compile time> && parser.position <
input.len() && !FIRST_BYTES.contains(&next_byte) { /* pruned: contributes nothing, do not touch best_* */ }` around
the existing body. Prunable branches with an empty/typed first-byte set get a `const`/`match`-based byte test
(cheap); non-prunable (`unresolved`/`nullable`) branches emit NO guard (unchanged codegen). The tournament selection
logic (`should_take`, best_end/priority/associativity) is UNTOUCHED ⇒ the longest_match winner among survivors is
identical, and pruned branches were guaranteed losers. Handle EOF (`position == len`) → only nullable branches
survive (already never-pruned). ⇒ byte-identical parse output; the equivalence/combinator/PCRE2 gates are the
soundness oracle for a too-tight first-set.

**F5 — the "shared exploration" ladder (director clarification, session #94).** The director's concurrency idea,
de-worded: NOT a parser/copy per branch, but **shared exploration** — scan the input ONCE, keep all still-viable
branches alive together, share the common work, split only at the point of divergence (the `flex`/`re2c` lexer /
GLR model: broadcast each char to every live alternative, drop the dead, fork at divergence). First-set dispatch is
RUNG 1 of exactly this: broadcast ONE char, prune the branches that can't match. For regex `atom` (~24 branches that
diverge at char 1 — `.`/`[`/`(`/`\`…, sharing NO prefix) one char of shared lookahead captures ~the whole win.
RUNG 2 (`.5.d`, general/parser-agnostic, FUTURE): when branches share a MULTI-char prefix (e.g. SV
`always_ff | always_comb | always_latch` sharing `always_`), scan the shared prefix ONCE and fork at divergence —
left-factoring / a prefix-DFA over the branch set (the tree's "DFA token layer" idea, technique #6). INVARIANT across
the whole ladder: the DFA/broadcast decides VIABILITY (who's still alive), but the WINNER among survivors is still
`longest_match` — never the first branch to finish (that would break parity, the `join_any` trap). Sequence: land
rung 1 (`.5.c`, sound/cheap/big regex win) → measure → climb to rung 2 (`.5.d`) where the profile shows shared-prefix
cost remains. (Opened as a forward pointer; `.5.d` is not yet a scheduled leaf — `.5.c` first.)

**F5b — NOVEL non-naive concurrency forms (director push, session #94: "parallelize in a NOVEL way, not naive; think
out-of-the-box").** The naive form (thread-per-branch) fails on granularity/state-clone/determinism — but two OTHER
concurrency forms fit this problem and are the deep rungs of the ladder; BOTH preserve the `longest_match` verdict
(concurrency decides VIABILITY, the tournament decides the WINNER):
- **(#1) SIMD data-parallelism (hardware concurrency, NO threads, deterministic, zero coordination cost)** — broadcast
  a CHUNK of input against ALL branch first-bytes / a small transition table in ONE vector instruction, returning a
  bitmask of still-viable branches. This IS "broadcast the search on all branches" done in parallel LANES. Model:
  Intel **Hyperscan** + **simdjson** structural classification. NOTE: `.4.b` proved the COMPILER won't
  auto-vectorize our branchy code ⇒ this must be HAND-WRITTEN SIMD for the specific "next-byte vs branch-set"
  op (a narrow, high-value kernel), not a `target-cpu` flag.
- **(#2) Algorithmic concurrency — ONE shared automaton carrying all live branches forward at once** (GLL/GLR /
  `re2c` graph-structured-stack / DFA): a single left-to-right scan advances EVERY branch simultaneously, shares the
  common prefix scan, splits only at divergence, re-merges at reconvergence ⇒ "all branches at once" costs
  O(shared + divergences), NOT N× malloc, with NO backtracking and NO per-branch state clone. This is the FULL,
  non-naive form of the director's "broadcast / split-at-divergence".
- **Why this is the WIN, not a detour:** Hyperscan + `re2c` (the hand-tuned C engines RGX-0078 races) ARE exactly
  forms #1+#2. So the SIMD-driven shared-automaton lexical fast path is where the PCRE2-relative gap actually closes
  (tree techniques #5 "specialized regex fast-path" + #6 "DFA token layer"). AMBITIOUS structural rung — a dedicated
  future slice with the general parser (`parse_harness_*`) as its correctness oracle, sequenced AFTER `.5.c` measures
  how far the cheap 1-char prune already gets us. Land rung 1 → measure → climb.

**F5c — the FULL lockstep-simulation DESTINATION (director + engineer AGREED, session #94).** Director: *"try all
branches at the same time, not one after the other… branches that would otherwise backtrack simply DIE, the one that
would match continues moving the cursor forward, until only one is left alive — and that's the right branch."*
ENGINEER: **AGREED — this is exactly Thompson's NFA simulation (1968) / RE2 / `re2c` / GLL** (Russ Cox, "Regular
Expression Matching Can Be Simple And Fast"): advance ALL alternatives in lockstep, dead ones drop in place, survivors
move the cursor forward together — **NO backtracking (nothing rewinds/retries) and NO per-speculation malloc (nothing
is cloned-then-rolled-back), linear time.** This is THE way to eliminate the backtracking + malloc at the ROOT (not
just reduce them), and it IS what the hand-tuned C engines RGX-0078 races actually do ⇒ the real path to closing the
PCRE2 gap. THREE signoff precisions (the idea is correct; these keep it sound + efficient):
  1. **Winner = longest-match, not literally "last alive".** Usually collapses to ONE survivor (the director's picture
     exactly), but when several branches COMPLETE, the winner is the LONGEST completed match (ties → earliest/priority)
     — POSIX leftmost-longest, how RE2 stays correct. Same verdict as today's tournament, computed by lockstep advance
     instead of serial retry ⇒ parity preserved (the ⛔ floor).
  2. **Semantic actions + AST building = the genuinely HARD part (the "signoff + efficient" crux the director named).**
     Our branches don't just match — each BUILDS a typed AST + can emit facts/open scopes. In lockstep, several live
     branches build DIFFERENT ASTs at once ⇒ each must accumulate its AST + semantic effects LAZILY (a pending buffer)
     and only the WINNER's are applied, while SHARING common sub-results across branches (a shared packed parse forest,
     GLL/SPPF). The whole win hinges on that sharing NOT re-introducing the per-branch malloc we're eliminating.
  3. **Store-gated branches stay live until their predicate resolves** (viability can depend on a `@predicate`, not just
     the next char) — conservative, keeps the model parser-agnostic (semantics, not only characters).
PARSER-AGNOSTIC SHAPE (honest scope): regular sublanguages (regex `atom`, keyword sets, any terminal choice) → a **DFA**
simulation, TRACTABLE NOW, captures the bulk of the regex win; recursive/structural alternations → **GLL** + a
graph-structured stack, the complete general form but RESEARCH-GRADE (an engine effort, not a weekend — stated honestly).

**F5d — HOW to solve precision #2 elegantly (per-branch AST + semantic effects WITHOUT re-introducing malloc; director
+ engineer co-design, session #94).** The crux ("each live branch needs its own AST/store, but K clones = the malloc we
are killing") dissolves into THREE known-elegant techniques — and PGEN ALREADY HOLDS MOST OF THE PIECES (compose, don't
invent):
  1. **AST → lazy materialization + structural sharing (SPPF).** A live branch records a CHEAP TRACE ("rule R matched
     span [i,j]" = a few integers appended to a shared log), NOT a constructed AST. The heavy AST is built ONCE, for the
     WINNER, at tournament end, by replaying its trace. Shared sub-parses POINT at one node (a DAG / shared packed parse
     forest), keyed `(rule, start, end)` — which is EXACTLY the **packrat memo key**. ⇒ **the memo IS the shared forest**;
     the director's "share the structure, allocate per-branch by some hash" — the hash already exists.
  2. **Semantic store → copy-on-write overlay, NOT K clones.** Each branch = `base_store + a small per-branch OVERLAY`
     (only the facts IT emitted), persistent/COW, epoch-stamped. Dead branch → drop overlay (near-free); winner → commit
     overlay onto base. Branches SHARE the base; only deltas are per-branch. PGEN ALREADY HAS the machinery:
     `extract_delta_since`, `SemanticRuntimeDelta`, the write-epoch-tainted memo (MEMO-STORE-SOUNDNESS.2). The director's
     "allocate only the per-live-branch part, indexed by branch" = the epoch-stamped overlay.
  3. **Allocation → one arena, mass-free.** Per-branch scratch lives in ONE arena; at tournament end move the winner's
     data out + reset — losers freed EN MASSE (one free, not K). This is the planned arena lever (technique #7), which
     COMPOSES with the simulation instead of fighting it.
  Synthesis: the "difficulties" are three primitives PGEN already speaks — **structural sharing** (the memo), **COW
  deltas** (the semantic runtime), **lazy materialization** (build the winner once). Not invention from zero —
  composition. The `parse_harness_*` differential is the correctness oracle: if the sharing ever lies, a byte diverges
  immediately. Lexical layer tractable soonest; full structural GLL is the real (honest) engine effort.
RELATION TO `.5.c`: first-set dispatch is the SOUND FIRST MILE of this exact road — the one-step degenerate case ("kill
the branches that die at char 1") — ships now, captures the big regex win, and de-risks the automaton. DISCIPLINE
([[feedback_correctness_before_speed]] + [[feedback_research_grounded_sota_no_trial_and_revert]]): land the safe
increment (`.5.c`) → MEASURE → build the DFA/simulation layer (`.5.d` lexical DFA → `.5.e` GLL) with that number in hand,
NOT open-heart engine surgery on a hypothesis. DESTINATION AGREED; `.5.c` is the first mile.

**Design decision (autonomous, within the speed-phase principles):** implement as `.5.c.2` — a shared FIRST-set
first-byte helper + a codegen guard in `generate_or_logic`, parser-agnostic, regen ALL parsers + verify regen, then
decisive drift-controlled `regex_perf_probe` + the full oracle battery under the ⛔ HARD CONSTRAINT. Sequenced as the
next slice (the design is now concrete + feasibility-proven; this scout slice commits the plan durably first).
**Considered + set aside (session #94, director brainstorm) — PER-BRANCH PARALLELISM ("launch all Or-branches at
once, each with its own cursor/state copy, take the first").** Right instinct (same "stop doing wasted work" as
`.5.c`), WRONG GRAIN for the inner tournament — three converging reasons, all tool-backed: (1) GRANULARITY — a branch
attempt is ns-scale (match one metachar), thread spawn/join is µs-scale → coordination costs 100–1000× the work
(same category error as "add a JIT", already ruled out — the unit of work is too small to amortize); (2) it
MULTIPLIES the bottleneck — each parallel branch needs its OWN clone of the cursor + semantic runtime (facts/scopes/
memo), i.e. N state-clones instead of 1, and the `.2` profile pins **59% malloc** on exactly that per-speculation
state-cloning ⇒ parallelism makes the root cause N× worse; (3) DETERMINISM (⛔ HARD floor) — "continue with the first
to succeed" is scheduling-dependent → non-deterministic winner → parity reject; a deterministic version must join ALL
branches + apply the longest_match tie-break, waiting for the slowest ⇒ no latency win. REFRAME: when work is tiny and
STATE is expensive to copy, ELIMINATING losing branches (first-set prune → 23/24 cost ZERO) beats PARALLELIZING them
(24× the malloc, merely wall-clock-overlapped) — so `.5.c` is the STRONGER form of the same instinct, not a weaker one.
`fork/join_any` REFINEMENT (director) makes the correctness break SHARPER, not softer: `join_any` returns the FIRST
branch to FINISH = the SHORTEST match (a literal returns in one step; a nested group runs longer), but the tournament
rule is LONGEST — so `join_any` systematically commits to the shorter match (`a|ab` on `"ab"` → picks `"a"`), a
wrong-winner PARITY break, not an efficiency wrinkle. Correct semantics needs `join_ALL` + longest tie-break ⇒ wait
for the slowest branch ⇒ no latency win, and all three grain/malloc/determinism costs remain. RUST-OWNERSHIP wall:
forking branches as threads = N tasks touching the parser's `&mut` cursor + semantic state ⇒ either CLONE per task
(multiplies the 59% malloc) or `Arc<Mutex>` (per-branch lock contention, catastrophic at ns granularity) — the
borrow checker states the same objection the profile does. LITERATURE ([[feedback_research_grounded_sota_no_trial_and_revert]]):
successful parallel parsers (parallel Earley; "parsing gigabytes of JSON in parallel"; speculative chunk parsing)
parallelize across INPUT REGIONS of a LARGE input, NEVER across one Or-node's branches — and a regex pattern is
dozens of bytes (nothing to chunk).

**★ PROMOTED to a tracked COARSE-GRAIN lever (`.7`, RGX/embedding-API side — the CORRECT home for concurrency):**
**one-whole-parse-per-task `fork/join`** batch compilation. RGX compiles MANY patterns; parsing pattern A vs B is
independent, deterministic per-pattern, shares no mutable state, and each task is ~300µs of REAL work — the exact
regime where `fork/join` thrives (work ≫ coordination cost). Natural partner of the parse-cache (cache the repeats,
`fork/join` the misses). This is `fork/join_any`'s proper grain (per-pattern, not per-branch). NOT an inner-tournament
change; an embedding-API / RGX-consumer lever sequenced with `.6`/the parse-cache — see `.7` below.

- ID: `.5.c.2`  Status: `done` (session #95, 2026-07-12 — LANDED: **−75% geomean (~4× faster), byte-identical**; see the Acceptance Checklist below) — IMPLEMENT the first-byte predictive-dispatch guard per F1–F4: shared
  FIRST-set first-byte helper (extract/adapt from `annotation_validator`, callable from codegen on the gen-AST
  `grammar_tree`) + emit the `branch_can_start` guard in `generate_or_logic`'s branch loop; regen ALL parsers +
  verify regen ([[feedback_verify_sv_parser_regen_mtime]]); decisive drift-controlled `regex_perf_probe`
  before→after (build BOTH fat-LTO, alternate) + the full oracle battery byte-identical (PCRE2 diff / cert seeds
  0/7/42 / equivalence / combinator incl. `a|ab` / semantic / duality / ast-shape / clippy). Land iff improves +
  byte-identical; any parity flip = REJECT + retighten to `ANY`.

#### `.5.c.2` DESIGN REFINEMENTS (session #95, tools-first, before any code — two soundness constraints F1–F4 under-specified)

**R1 — the guard is emitted ONLY for a RULE-TOP-LEVEL `Or`, not a nested one (furthest_position soundness).**
`furthest_position` is bumped ONLY at rule entry (`ast_based_generator.rs:3256` — `if self.position > self.furthest_position`),
NEVER inside `match_string`. A pruned branch fails at char 1 = `parse_start`; the MOST it could push into
`furthest_position` is `parse_start` (reached iff it enters ≥1 child rule there). For a **rule-top-level `Or`**,
`parse_start` == the rule's own entry position, which the rule-entry bump already folded into `furthest_position`
(the wrappers between the bump and the body — memo/transaction/`push_rule_context` — do NOT touch `position`), so
`furthest_position ≥ parse_start` ALREADY ⇒ pruning is furthest-neutral BY CONSTRUCTION. For a **nested `Or`** (after
some matched terminals) `parse_start` can exceed the rule-entry position while `furthest_position` still sits at the
entry (terminals advance `position` without bumping `furthest`); pruning a rule-ref-leading nested branch that today
would enter a child at `parse_start` (bumping `furthest` to `parse_start`) could then LEAVE `furthest` lower ⇒ a
`furthest_position` divergence on reject (the combinator/equivalence gates check it). Restricting to top-level `Or`s
preserves the ENTIRE primary win (regex `atom := literal | dot | … | group` IS a rule body ⇒ top-level; the `.5`
profile's `parse_atom` 7565-sample dominator) and is provably byte-identical incl. furthest. Nested-`Or` pruning is a
future increment (needs the furthest-bump-mirroring worked out, or the `.5.d` lockstep automaton).
- ID: `.5.c.2.a`  Status: `queued` (forward pointer) — extend first-set pruning to NESTED `Or`s by emitting a
  furthest-correcting bump in the prune path (only for branches whose leading first-set path enters a rule at
  `parse_start`). Deferred: the primary win is fully captured by top-level `Or`s; do it only if the profile shows
  nested-`Or` tournament cost remaining after `.5.c.2` measures.

**R2 — the guard is gated on `self.layout_sensitivity().terminals` (terminal whitespace-sensitivity).** `match_string`
skips leading layout via `consume_layout_for_terminal` when `#allow_layout_skip_for_terminals` (`= !layout_sensitivity.terminals`).
Peeking the RAW byte at `parse_start` is sound ONLY when NO terminal layout-skip happens first (else a branch whose
terminal would match AFTER skipping whitespace would be wrongly pruned). So emit iff `layout_sensitivity().terminals == true`
(regex is `@whitespace_sensitive: true` ⇒ emitted; SV/json layout-skipping ⇒ NOT emitted, byte-identical, no regression,
no win there). Capability-gated (declared layout policy), NOT grammar-name-gated ⇒ [[feedback_features_parser_agnostic_enable_all_parsers]]-compliant.

**R3 — plumbing: `grammar_tree` is threaded as a param, NOT a field, and `generate_or_logic` doesn't receive it.**
Snapshot it onto `self` via interior mutability (`RefCell<HashMap<String, ASTNode>>`, mirroring the existing
`uses_match_regex: Cell<bool>` precedent) at the `&self` generation entry, read it in `generate_or_logic`. Avoids
threading `grammar_tree` through ~8 `generate_*` fns. First-set analysis extracted to a new shared module
`ast_pipeline/first_set.rs` (moved verbatim from `annotation_validator.rs`; the four fns are pure — never read
`self.config` — so the move is behavior-preserving; the ebnf/annotation gates confirm). The interpreter needs NO change:
pruning is output-neutral, so the generated parser WITH pruning still equals the interpreter WITHOUT it (that IS why the
equivalence/combinator gates stay green — they are the soundness oracle for a too-tight first-set).
