### `.5` RE-PROFILE #2 (session #96, 2026-07-12) — post-`.5.c.2`; steer the next lever (arena vs nested-`Or`)

**Why (mandated precondition).** `.5.c.2` (first-set dispatch, **−75%**) pruned ~23/24 `atom` branches per char, so the
`.2`/`.5` profile (which attributed 59% malloc largely to the losing-branch tournament) is STALE. Before choosing the
next structural, correctness-risky lever, re-profile the CURRENT committed WITH-FIX binary (per
[[feedback_no_codebase_change_without_tool_backed_facts]] + TOOLBOX-first). Profiled the exact committed release
binary (sha256 `e2c28021…`, == the `.5.c.2`-recorded with-fix sha) via macOS `sample` (12 s @1 ms) over the
`regex_perf_probe` hot loop; the on-disk `generated/regex_parser.rs` was first regenerated (`focus_regex`) to carry the
committed 820 first-set guards (it had been a STALE gitignored artifact — confirmed benign, the committed codegen
`first_set.rs` + `generate_or_logic` guard emission are intact). Fresh current geomean-of-mins (2000/200):
**74,577 ns ≈ 74.6µs** (consistent with the `.5.c.2`-pinned ~76µs — the arena before-number).

**Leaf self-time categorization (9818 samples, `sample` "Sort by top of stack"):**

| Bucket | Share | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| **malloc / free / memops** | **55.7%** | `_xzm_free` 1717, `_xzm_xzone_malloc` 578, `_malloc_zone_malloc` 500, `_platform_memmove` 441, `_free` 406, `<dedup malloc>` 756, memset/bzero/realloc — STILL dominant (was 59%) |
| parse / match | 13.6% | the surviving branches' real work — `parse_atom`/`parse_letter`/`parse_piece`/`parse_literal_char` closures |
| drop | 8.2% | `drop_in_place<ParseNode>`/`<ParseContent>`/`<serde_json::Value>`/`<SemanticFactRecord>` + BTreeMap `dying_next` 183 |
| clone | 6.6% | `ParseNode::clone` 112, `ParseContent::clone` 90, `Vec::clone`, BTreeMap `clone_subtree` 140 |
| semantic-runtime | 4.6% | `directives_for_rule` 156 (FxHashed residual = slice-return + branch-classify), `rollback_to_named` 100, `extract_delta_since` 63 |
| hashmap / sip | 4.3% | residual SipHash `write` 155 + hashbrown insert/rehash (memo) |
| probe-timing (getenv/clock) | 3.1% | `mach_absolute_time` 253 + `__findenv_locked` 44 — the BENCH's own per-parse clock reads, **NOT parse cost** (a measurement artifact now visible at 76µs) |
| string-fmt | 2.1% | DOWN from 6% — the C3-B cleanup `format!` strings on losing branches are now pruned away (as `.5.c` predicted) |

**Conclusion (tool-backed steering — CONFIRMS the agreed road).** The −75% win removed losing-branch work PROPORTIONALLY, so
**allocation is still the dominant cost**: malloc 55.7% + drop 8.2% + clone 6.6% ≈ **70% of self-time is memory-management of
parse structures**. This CONFIRMS the agreed F5c/F5d destination: the residual allocation is EXACTLY what the lockstep
simulation eliminates at the root — F5d piece 1 (lazy materialization / memo-as-SPPF: build the winner's AST once, share it)
+ piece 3 (one arena, mass-free). Per the agreed sequence (`.5.c` → MEASURE → `.5.d`), the next slice is the **`.5.d`
lexical-DFA / lockstep-simulation DESIGN SPIKE (`.5.d.1`)**, with the arena/SPPF/COW as its COMPOSING PIECES — **NOT** a
standalone arena lever (a standalone arena only *pools* the malloc; the lockstep road *eliminates* it, and is the one whose
ceiling reaches PCRE2 parity). Nested-`Or` pruning (`.5.c.2.a`) stays DEFERRED (attacks only the 13.6% `parse/match` bucket).
The string-fmt lever (`.5.b`) is FORMALLY MOOT (2.1%, its losing-branch `format!`s pruned). Design inputs for `.5.d.1`: the
malloc SOURCE is WINNER AST construction + the memo clone-in/clone-out + tournament candidate clones (heavy `ParseNode::clone`
/ `ParseContent::clone` / BTreeMap `clone_subtree` in the leaf table) — precisely the eager-build-and-clone pattern the
memo-as-SPPF design removes; a residual SipHash `write` (155 leaf ≈ 1.5%, likely the `fact_kinds`/`predicate_defs` std maps
`.5.a` deferred) is a tiny OPTIONAL side-cleanup, not the prize. Raw dump: session scratchpad `reprofile2.txt`. Docs-only
steering slice — no code change (the profile INFORMS the agreed `.5.d` design; every `.5.d` code slice re-runs the full ⛔
HARD-CONSTRAINT oracle battery before landing).

### COURSE CORRECTION (session #96, 2026-07-12) — realign to the AGREED F5c/F5d road; the standalone `.5.f` was an off-agreement drift

**What happened.** After the RE-PROFILE #2 (the agreed MEASURE step, correct), the first write-up opened a STANDALONE `.5.f`
"arena / clone-elision" lever framed as "the confirmed #1 remaining lever," disconnected from the AGREED F5c/F5d destination —
because the profiler headline ("malloc still 55.7%") was matched to the tree's brainstorm *technique menu* (#7 "arena = the
single biggest allocation lever") instead of being read against the agreed plan, where the arena is **piece #3 of the `.5.d`
lockstep simulation**, not a separate lever. The director caught it and reaffirmed: stick to what we agreed; a drift can kill
the goal.

**The correction (this section + the `.5.d`/`.5.e`/`.5.f` leaf edits above).** The agreement STANDS unchanged: destination =
the full lockstep simulation (advance all branches together, eliminate backtracking + per-speculation malloc at the ROOT — the
only road whose ceiling reaches PCRE2 parity, exactly what re2c/Hyperscan do); sequence `.5.c` (done) → MEASURE (done,
RE-PROFILE #2) → `.5.d` lexical DFA → `.5.e` GLL. The arena is NOT discarded — it is re-folded as F5d COMPOSING PIECE #3 of
`.5.d`. The RE-PROFILE #2 data is correct and REINFORCES the road (the residual malloc is the eager-build-and-clone pattern the
memo-as-SPPF design eliminates). Next slice = the `.5.d.1` design spike, on the agreed road.

**Durable lesson.** New data (a profile) steers work WITHIN an agreed road; it never silently overrides the agreed
sequencing/destination. Any proposed deviation must be flagged LOUDLY, with its reason, BEFORE acting — never drifted into
silently. Recorded as [[feedback_stick_to_agreed_plan_no_silent_drift]]. Orient to the agreed GOAL: accuracy stable (the floor,
mechanically enforced by the `parse_harness_*` byte-identical differential) + PCRE2-parity speed (reached by the lockstep road).

### `.5.d.1` DESIGN SPIKE (session #96, 2026-07-12) — the lockstep simulation, decomposed into byte-identical increments

Docs-first, NO code. The agreed `.5.d` first design (F5c/F5d) — tool-backed by the engine-machinery survey (references
below are verified against `ast_based_generator.rs` codegen templates, `semantic_runtime.rs`, `mod.rs`, and the emitted
`generated/regex_parser.rs`). GOAL: eliminate the RE-PROFILE #2 dominant cost (~70% build/clone/free of parse structures) at
the ROOT via the lockstep simulation — the road whose ceiling reaches PCRE2 parity.

#### A. What the survey found (the ACTUAL machinery) — two findings reframe the effort

**A1 — the tournament is ALREADY half-lockstep (F5d piece 2 is shipped).** `generate_or_logic`
(`ast_based_generator.rs:3495`; multi-branch `3582`–`4113`) already: attempts every branch from ONE shared baseline
(`parse_start` + `tournament_semantic_checkpoint`, `4024`/`4039`), captures each branch's semantic mutation as a DELTA
(`extract_delta_since(&tournament_semantic_checkpoint)`, `3925`), rolls the store back per branch (`rollback_to_named`,
`3937`), keeps only the WINNER's delta (`best_semantic_delta`, `3955`), and applies ONLY the winner's delta once at the end
(`apply_delta`, `4096`–`4100`). That IS F5d piece 2 (the epoch-stamped COW store overlay) — `SemanticRuntimeDelta`
(`semantic_runtime.rs:1813`) + `apply_delta` (`2659`, bumps `write_epoch` only on observable mutation) + the
`tainted_at_epoch` memo taint (MEMO-STORE-SOUNDNESS.2). We are NOT inventing the hard semantic part; it exists.

**A2 — the waste is pinpointed and SEPARABLE.** (i) The return-annotation transform (JSON folding via
`ParseContent::to_json_value`, `mod.rs:731`) runs EAGERLY, PER BRANCH ATTEMPT — `let transformed = { let content =
raw_content.clone(); #transform };` (`ast_based_generator.rs:3735`–`3738`) — so a 24-branch `atom` does **24 folds + 24
`raw_content` clones and keeps 1** (`best_content = transformed` only on win, `3952`). (ii) The packrat memo
(`memoized_call`, `regex_parser.rs:356559`; key `(RuleId, start)` ONLY, `356569`) DEEP-CLONES the whole `ParseNode` subtree
on INSERT (`356691`) AND again on every HIT (`356658`), plus `semantic_delta.clone()` on hit (`356648`). These two are the
RE-PROFILE #2 70% clone/malloc, now located exactly.

#### B. The three F5d pieces, mapped to the code (verified)

- **Piece 2 — COW store overlay: SHIPPED** (A1). Reuse `extract_delta_since`/`apply_delta`/`SemanticRuntimeDelta`/epoch as-is.
  (Residual inefficiency: the delta's `.to_vec()` clones at `semantic_runtime.rs:2619`/`2624`/`2640` — small vs the AST cost.)
- **Piece 1 — lazy materialization + sharing: PARTIAL.** The memo already caches `(rule,start) → one ParseNode` (correct for a
  DETERMINISTIC PEG — a true `(rule,start,end)` SPPF is only needed for AMBIGUOUS parses, i.e. `.5.e`/GLL, NOT `.5.d`). The two
  GAPS are: (i) the transform is materialized eagerly per attempt (A2-i) instead of once for the winner; (ii) the memo shares
  by DEEP CLONE instead of by reference (A2-ii). Both are `.5.d` targets.
- **Piece 3 — arena: NEW.** `ParseContent`/`ParseNode` (`mod.rs:715`/`755`) use `Box`/`Vec` per node (`Terminal(&'input str)`
  already zero-copy). Bump-allocate nodes in one arena; winner retained, losers freed en masse. Must respect the `'input` lifetime.

#### C. The creative decomposition — the lockstep road as byte-identical increments (each a piece of the agreed sim, NOT a standalone lever)

- **`.5.d.2` — LAZY WINNER-ONLY MATERIALIZATION (F5d piece 1, first mile; the big quick win).** Defer the JSON-fold transform
  (`#transform` + `raw_content.clone()`) from PER-BRANCH (`3735`) to WINNER-ONLY. Selection (`should_take`, `3865`–`3913`) needs
  only `candidate_end`/`candidate_priority`/`branch_predicate_blocked` — NOT the transformed output — so the winner is picked
  from the cheap `raw_content` (mostly zero-copy `Terminal(&str)`), then the transform runs ONCE in the winner-resolution block
  (`4074`+, where `parser.position` is already restored to `best_end == candidate_end`, satisfying the `$text`/MatchedText
  `input[start..position]` ordering constraint the eager placement exists for — comment `3726`–`3734`). Kills "fold 24, keep 1".
  BYTE-IDENTICAL BY CONSTRUCTION: the winner's transform yields the same output deferred vs eager (same `raw_content`, same
  `position`); losers' transforms are discarded today (`3952`) so not running them is output-neutral — the exact soundness family
  as `.5.b`/`.5.c`. ⚠️ CONSTRAINT: some branch predicates consume `transformed` (`3768`–`3812`), so defer ONLY for branches whose
  predicate does not read the transform (a capability check, like `.5.c`'s guard) — still covers the predicate-free `atom` branches
  that dominate the profile.
- **`.5.d.3` — MEMO SUBTREE SHARING (F5d piece 1 cont. + piece 3).** Replace the memo deep-clone-on-insert (`356691`) +
  deep-clone-on-hit (`356658`) with a SHARED handle — `Rc<ParseNode>` or an arena index — so a hit returns a cheap shared
  reference. Kills the single biggest clone source. Byte-identical: a shared immutable subtree is observationally identical to a
  cloned one (the AST is not mutated after construction). Interacts with the `'input` lifetime + the arena (piece 3).
- **`.5.d.4` — THE LOCKSTEP DFA ADVANCE (the core).** Replace the SERIAL try-each-branch (`try_parse` + per-branch
  `rollback_to_named`, `3710`/`3937`) with a SINGLE forward scan over the branch set for terminal/regular choice sites (regex
  `atom` = regex.ebnf:365): broadcast the next byte to all live branches, drop the dead in place (no rewind), record completions;
  WINNER = longest completed match with the EXISTING `best_end`/priority/associativity tie-break (`3892`–`3913`) — the DFA decides
  VIABILITY, the tournament rule decides the WINNER ⇒ parity. Terminal-only branches match fully; group-family branches
  (`group`/`lookaround`/... all start `(`) discriminate their introducer prefix in one shared scan, then recurse into
  recursive-descent (which re-enters `.5.d` at its own choices). The store COW (piece 2) + lazy materialization (`.5.d.2`) +
  sharing (`.5.d.3`) compose in. No backtracking, no per-speculation malloc.
- **`.5.e` — GLL + true `(rule,start,end)` SPPF** for recursive/structural + ambiguous alternations (the complete general form,
  research-grade).

#### D. The three F5c precisions (preserved by every increment)

1. **Winner = longest-match, NOT "last alive."** Keep `best_end`/`should_take`/priority/associativity (`3865`–`3913`) unchanged —
   every increment changes HOW branches are explored, never the winner RULE. (`nonassoc_tie` → `Err(Backtrack)` at `4069` preserved.)
2. **Semantic actions + AST = lazy.** `.5.d.2` makes the AST/JSON lazy (winner-only); piece 2 already makes store effects
   winner-only (apply only `best_semantic_delta`). Losers materialize nothing.
3. **Store-gated liveness.** A branch whose viability depends on a `@predicate` (not just the next char) stays live until the
   predicate resolves — the branch-predicate + `phase:final` obligation machinery (`3740`–`3864`, `new_obligations`) already does this.

#### E. Soundness / byte-identical oracle (the ⛔ HARD-CONSTRAINT floor) + open risks

- Oracle per increment: the interpreter (unchanged, serial) vs the generated parser (the increment) via
  `parse_harness_equivalence_gate` (verdict + AST + `furthest_position`, ALL grammars, seeds 0/7/42) + `parse_harness_combinator_gate`
  (the `a|ab` longest-match discrimination — the exact case a lockstep bug breaks) + semantic 36/36 + regex cert `fully_certified` +
  PCRE2 compile-oracle diff + duality + ast-shape + clippy + all-9-parsers byte-identical regen. Same battery as `.5.c`. Any flip = REJECT.
- ⚠️ OPEN RISKS the implementation must resolve (design flags them now): (R-a) **`furthest_position` preservation** — the lockstep
  "drop in place" must reach the same per-branch max byte the serial try's furthest would (`furthest_position` bumped at rule entry,
  `ast_based_generator.rs:3256`); verify the DFA advance records it identically (the equivalence/combinator gates check furthest).
  (R-b) **predicate/transform coupling** (`.5.d.2`) — defer only where the branch predicate does not consume `transformed`. (R-c)
  **effect ORDERING** — the winner's effects must apply at the SAME logical point (post-selection, `4096`) as today. (R-d) **`'input`
  lifetime × arena/Rc** (`.5.d.3`).
- **UNFORESEEN-ISSUE PROTOCOL (director 2026-07-12).** R-a..R-d are PRE-REGISTERED pause-and-agree points, and the same holds for
  any NEW unforeseen issue that surfaces mid-implementation: **PAUSE → analyze it → reflect → propose + AGREE a solution WITH the
  director → then proceed.** No unilateral solve-and-press-on (that is how drift starts). Be creative/inventive when proposing the
  fix, but agree before acting. [[feedback_stick_to_agreed_plan_no_silent_drift]].

#### F. Parser-agnostic + sequencing

Codegen/engine primitive gated on CAPABILITY (a choice site whose branches are DFA-discriminable / predicate-free-transform), never
grammar name → every grammar inherits it ([[feedback_features_parser_agnostic_enable_all_parsers]]). Sequence: `.5.d.2`
(lazy materialization, biggest quick win, lowest risk) → `.5.d.3` (memo sharing) → `.5.d.4` (DFA advance) → `.5.e` (GLL). Each:
design → decisive drift-controlled `regex_perf_probe` before→after → full oracle battery → land iff faster + byte-identical.

**Children (opened):** `.5.d.2` (lazy winner-only materialization — NEXT), `.5.d.3` (memo subtree sharing), `.5.d.4` (lockstep DFA advance).

### `.5.d.2` IMPLEMENTATION (session #97, 2026-07-12) — LAZY WINNER-ONLY MATERIALIZATION — ⛔ REVERTED (idea preserved)

> **STATUS: REVERTED.** The codegen below was built, proven 100% byte-identical, measured SPEED-NEUTRAL, then reverted
> (`git checkout` of `ast_based_generator.rs` back to the `.5.c.2` baseline) at the director's steer. The elegant TOOL and the
> **substitutes** LESSON are preserved durably in [[project_lazy_winner_only_materialization_lesson]] (and this section is kept
> as the worked design so the tool can be re-derived for `.5.d.4`). Nothing landed on `main`.


**The change (codegen, parser-AGNOSTIC, `rust/src/ast_pipeline/ast_based_generator.rs` `generate_or_logic`):** in the
multi-branch tournament, the per-branch return-annotation transform (`let transformed = { let content = raw_content.clone();
#transform };`, the eager site) is now DEFERRED to run ONLY for a winning-candidate branch, for rules that carry NO
branch-phase `@predicate`. Two shared codegen pieces:
1. `should_take_selection` — the winner-selection expression extracted to ONE token stream, interpolated identically into
   the eager and lazy branch bodies, so both explore branches under exactly the same longest-match / priority / associativity
   rule (only the transform TIMING differs). Guarantees the tournament decision is a single source of truth.
2. `defer_transform = self.rule_has_no_semantic_annotations(rule_name)` — the capability gate resolving open risk **R-b**
   (predicate/transform coupling). A rule with no runtime-relevant semantic annotation cannot carry a branch-phase predicate,
   so its branch-predicate loop is a runtime no-op and `transformed` is consulted by NOTHING except the winner assignment ⇒
   deferring is output-neutral. Such rules take the new `lazy_arm_inner` (decide `should_take` first — it never reads the
   transform — then run the transform + clone ONLY if `should_take`, BEFORE the position rollback so `$text`/MatchedText still
   slices `input[parse_start..candidate_end]` per BRANCH-BROADCAST-FIX.3); every other rule takes the byte-identical
   `eager_arm_inner`. The C3-B per-branch delta extract + rollback stay UNCONDITIONAL in both paths (store cleanup unchanged).

**MECHANISM NOTE (transparency — a within-goal realization choice, surfaced to the director).** The `.5.d.1` design sketch
said "run the transform ONCE in the winner-resolution block (`4074`+)". That literal placement needs a per-branch transform
RE-DISPATCH at `4074` (a `match best_branch_index { … }`) because `#transform` is **per-branch** (`ast_based_generator.rs`
~3686, keyed by rule+idx) — different branches fold different shapes. I realized the SAME goal (never transform a losing
branch) more simply and with lower risk via the `should_take` gate: the transform runs only for a *winning-candidate* branch
(K≈1–3 times, the number of times `best` improves), NOT once. The dominant profiled cost — the LOSING successful branches
that fold-and-discard ("fold 24, keep 1") — is eliminated identically by both. This keeps the transform per-branch-local (no
fragile index re-dispatch) and preserves the `$text` ordering naturally. Byte-identical either way. This is a mechanism
realization WITHIN the agreed `.5.d.2` goal, documented here per [[feedback_stick_to_agreed_plan_no_silent_drift]] +
[[feedback_surface_insights_prominently]]; if the director prefers strict once-at-`4074`, it is a follow-on refinement.

**Codegen split CONFIRMED in the regenerated `generated/regex_parser.rs` (tool-backed):** `902` branch sites took the lazy
body (`let transformed: Option<ParseContent<'input>> = if should_take`), `91` kept the eager body (`let transformed = {`);
the profile-dominant `parse_atom` and `parse_piece` both LAZY (verified by function-scoped grep). Parser-agnostic: every
grammar's semantically-inert multi-branch rules inherit the lazy path (proven by regenerating all 11 registered grammars).

#### Acceptance Checklist (enforced)
- [x] **REPRODUCE / ISSUE** — RE-PROFILE #2 (`.5` block): ~70% of regex parse self-time is build/clone/free of parse
  structures; the `.5.d.1` survey pinpointed the eager per-branch transform (`ast_based_generator.rs:3735`, `ParseContent::to_json_value`
  JSON fold via `mod.rs:731`) running for EVERY successful branch and keeping 1 ("fold 24, keep 1").
- [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — the return-annotation transform + `raw_content.clone()` at the eager site
  (`ast_based_generator.rs` `generate_or_logic`, the `let transformed = { let content = raw_content.clone(); #transform };`
  block) runs UNCONDITIONALLY per successfully-parsed branch; `should_take` (the winner decision) reads only
  `candidate_end`/`candidate_priority`/`branch_predicate_blocked`, never `transformed` — confirmed by reading the tournament
  codegen (the only pre-winner consumer of `transformed` is the branch-predicate loop, `branch_predicates_for_rule*`).
- [x] **FIX** — codegen (`ast_based_generator.rs`, tier: shared parser-agnostic codegen primitive). Defer the transform to
  winning-candidate-only for rules gated by `rule_has_no_semantic_annotations` (sound R-b resolution: no branch predicate ⇒
  `transformed` unused pre-winner). Shared `should_take_selection` token keeps winner-selection identical across both paths.
- [x] **ADDRESSED (verified) — CONCLUDED as a verified NEGATIVE: NO speed-win earned ⇒ FALSIFIED + REVERTED** (the `[x]` marks
  the investigation done + acted-upon, NOT a win — the lever did NOT land). ⚠️ The change is byte-identical but SPEED-NEUTRAL (NO
  measurable win). Decisive
  drift-controlled `regex_perf_probe` geomean-of-mins, BOTH release fat-LTO binaries (distinct sha256 `15958476…` fix / `449faa8b…`
  nofix; nofix regen confirmed EAGER = 0 lazy sites, fix = 902), measured ALTERNATELY, **7 rounds**: fix mean ≈ **75.5µs**, nofix mean
  ≈ **75.9µs** — the two OVERLAP heavily (per-round sign flips: fix slower R1/R4, faster R5/R6/R7; inter-round variance ~74.6–77.1µs
  EXCEEDS the fix↔nofix gap). This does NOT clear the decisive-separation bar the prior levers cleared (`.5.a` had zero overlap). ⇒
  NO speed-win earned; this leaf's acceptance is closed as a verified negative and the lever was REVERTED to the `.5.c.2` baseline.
  Root cause (tool-backed by the null itself): removing ALL losing-branch transforms
  (fix) yields no speedup vs keeping them (nofix) ⇒ their cost is negligible — because **`.5.c.2` first-set predictive dispatch
  already prunes the losing branches BEFORE they are tried/transformed**, so few "losing successful branches" remain to defer. The
  RE-PROFILE #2 ~70% clone/malloc is therefore elsewhere (the memo deep-clones `.5.d.3` targets + winner-path ParseNode/Value
  construction), NOT the losing-branch transform. **The `.5.d.2` premise ("the big quick win") is FALSIFIED post-`.5.c.2`.**
- [x] **NO REGRESSION** — ⛔ HARD constraint SATISFIED — byte-identical, every oracle green at its pre-opt value: `parse_harness_equivalence_gate`
  (interp↔generated byte-identical, all 11 grammars regen'd, seeds 0/7/42 — `certified_grammars_are_byte_identical` PASS) +
  `parse_harness_combinator_gate` (27/27 incl. `a|ab` longest-match) + `parse_harness_semantic_gate` (36/36 CLEAN) + regex cert
  `fully_certified` UNKNOWN=0 spf `0/1/1` seeds 0/7/42 (== pinned) + **PCRE2 fix-vs-nofix per-case diff EMPTY over 2188 cells
  (`1878/310/262/48` == pinned, 80-deep-paren line_4674 excluded)** + `duality_hunt_gate` (no new sig) + `regex_ast_shape_contract_gate`
  + source clippy exit 0 (generated debt pre-existing).
- [x] **LOCKSTEP** — DONE (revert path): code reverted to `.5.c.2` baseline; the tool + substitutes lesson preserved in
  [[project_lazy_winner_only_materialization_lesson]] (decision record) + INDEX + this leaf + the `inside-parser-performance.md`
  book note + CHANGES/DEVELOPMENT_NOTES/MEMORY. No contract/schema/ledger bump (nothing landed on `main`).

#### ⚠️ FINDING (session #97) — `.5.d.2` is byte-identical but SPEED-NEUTRAL; the design premise is falsified post-`.5.c.2`

The lazy winner-only transform is fully proven byte-identical (equivalence + combinator + semantic + cert + PCRE2 per-case diff all
green) but delivers NO measurable speedup (7-round decisive null, above). WHY: `.5.c.2` first-set predictive dispatch already skips
losing branches before they parse/transform, so the "fold 24 keep 1" cost the `.5.d.1` survey attributed to the eager transform is
already gone — the transform now runs for ≈the winner only even in the EAGER path. The RE-PROFILE #2 ~70% clone/malloc lives in the
MEMO deep-clones (`.5.d.3`: `memoized_call` deep-clone-on-insert `356691` / -on-hit `356658`) + winner-path node construction, which
this lever does not touch. Consequences: (a) per the SPEED-PHASE HARD CONSTRAINT + precedent (`.4.c` PGO not-landed for a marginal
win, `.4.b` reverted), a byte-identical-but-not-faster change does not land as a speed lever; (b) `.5.d.3` (memo subtree sharing) is
now the tool-indicated next lever; (c) lazy materialization is still architecturally REQUIRED by `.5.d.4` (a lockstep DFA advance
cannot transform per-branch), so it can be re-introduced there when it actually composes into a win. **DECISION SURFACED to the
director (land-as-foundational vs revert-and-re-sequence to `.5.d.3`); not decided unilaterally per [[feedback_stick_to_agreed_plan_no_silent_drift]]
+ [[feedback_be_alert_root_cause_fishy_immediately]].**

### `.5.d.3` RE-PROFILE #3 (session #98, 2026-07-12) — the `.5.d.3` premise is FALSIFIED before building; toolbox-first, applying the `.5.d.2` lesson

**Why (the `.5.d.2` lesson, applied).** `.5.d.3`'s status line reads "the `.5.d.2` null proves the RE-PROFILE #2 ~70% clone/malloc is the MEMO deep-clones." That is an **inference**, not a measurement — and the durable `.5.d.2` lesson ([[project_lazy_winner_only_materialization_lesson]]) is *before landing a speed lever, MEASURE the marginal gain against the CURRENT baseline; do not infer*. So, per [[project_uvm_memory_not_the_memo]] ("profile, don't infer") + TOOLBOX-first, I profiled the memo-clone cost SPECIFICALLY **before** building the (high-risk, `'input`-lifetime × `Rc`/arena) `.5.d.3` change.

**Tool 1 — `PGEN_REPORT_MEMO_STATS` (the memo footprint is tiny).** Over the 8 `regex_perf_probe` patterns (`parseability_probe --parse regex`, the eager/main `.5.c.2` baseline binary sha `449faa8b…`): the LARGEST memo footprint is character_class at **396 subtree-nodes across 218 success entries = avg 1.8 nodes/entry** (mostly `Terminal(&str)` leaves — a non-allocating pointer copy on clone). Biggest single memo entry ≈ 8 nodes (`descending_class_range`). anchor_complex 231 nodes / capture_groups 169 / email_basic 85. The clone-on-INSERT volume the memo pays is therefore small and cheap.

**Tool 2 — macOS `sample` (40 s @1 ms, 33,671 leaf samples, same binary), self-time buckets:**

| Bucket | Share | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| **malloc + free + memops** | **54.6%** (malloc 24.4 / free 16.8 / memmove+memcmp+memset+bzero 13.4) | broad allocation of ALL parse structures (winner AST + serde_json Values + Vecs + Strings). Only the ARENA (mass allocation) + construction-elimination attacks this at the root. |
| parse work | 17.5% | the surviving branches' real work (`parse_atom`/`parse_piece`/`parse_letter`/`match_string`/`::new` closures) |
| drop | 6.9% | `drop_in_place<serde_json::Value>` 491 + BTreeMap `dying_next` 528 + `[ParseNode]`/`ParseNode`/`ParseContent` drops |
| semantic-runtime | 4.4% | `directives_for_rule` 503 + `rollback_to_named` 469 + `extract_delta_since` 151 |
| clone | 4.2% | BTreeMap `clone_subtree` 574 (**serde_json Value::Object**, ancestry = `to_json_value`/`parse_simple_escape`) + `Vec::clone` 323 + `ParseNode::clone` 291 + `ParseContent::clone` 137 |
| **`split_semantic_top_level`** | **2.8%** (930) | RE-SPLITTING the FIXED predicate-expression string on `||`/`&&`/6 comparison ops per predicate EVALUATION (`evaluate_relational_expression_inner`) — a pure repeated recomputation |
| hash | 1.7% | residual SipHash/hashbrown (memo) |
| probe timing | 1.5% | `mach_absolute_time` — the bench's per-parse clock reads, NOT parse cost |

**THE FINDING (decisive, tool-backed).** The `.5.d.3`-as-specified DIRECT target — `ParseNode::clone` (0.86%) + `ParseContent::clone` (0.41%) = **1.27% of self-time**, and only the memo-attributable FRACTION of that (most memo entries are `Terminal(&str)` leaves whose clone does not even allocate). This is the **same speed-neutral territory as `.5.d.2`** — well under the decisive-separation bar every landed lever cleared. The `.5.d.3` premise ("memo deep-clones are the single biggest clone source") is **FALSIFIED**: the biggest clone item is actually serde_json `Value::Object`/BTreeMap (the WINNER's return-annotation JSON transform, ancestry `to_json_value`), which `.5.d.3` does not touch. The real cost is **broad construction malloc (54.6%)**, fixable only by the ARENA + construction-elimination — i.e. the `.5.d.4` lockstep advance (piece 3), NOT standalone memo node-sharing.

**Consequence — `.5.d.3` (memo NODE sharing) alone is a predicted ~1% null**, exactly like `.5.d.2`; its real value is as a COMPOSING PIECE of `.5.d.4` (where the arena/Rc handle + the lockstep advance together eliminate the 54.6% at the root). This mirrors `.5.d.2` (lazy materialization → folded into `.5.d.4`). Two profile-indicated levers now stand out:
- **The arena / `.5.d.4` lockstep advance** — the agreed destination's core; the ONLY thing that moves the 54.6% construction malloc. Big + high-risk (R-a..R-d).
- **Predicate-expression PRECOMPILE** (NEW, off the `.5.d` sequence) — parse the FIXED relational predicate expression to an AST ONCE (at annotation-compile time) and evaluate the AST, instead of re-splitting the string on every evaluation. Attacks `split_semantic_top_level` 2.8% + the surrounding `evaluate_relational_expression_inner` re-parse cascade + its Vec malloc. Byte-identical (same split result), low-risk, parser-agnostic (every predicate-using grammar inherits it). A clean bankable win while `.5.d.4` is designed.

**⛔ DECISION SURFACED to the director, NOT decided unilaterally** ([[feedback_stick_to_agreed_plan_no_silent_drift]] — the anti-drift + UNFORESEEN-ISSUE protocol: a queued leaf's premise falsified by a fresh profile changes the agreed next step, so PAUSE → analyze → propose → AGREE, before acting). Options surfaced: (A, recommended) advance to `.5.d.4` (fold `.5.d.3`/`.5.d.2` in as pieces) — the agreed core, the only 54.6% lever; (B) bank the byte-identical predicate-precompile detour first, then `.5.d.4`; (C) build `.5.d.3` arena/Rc scaffolding as a standalone step anyway (predicted ~1% null, but it is the `.5.d.4` foundation). Raw data: session scratchpad `sample_memo.txt`. Docs-only steering slice — no code change.

### `.5.d.4` DESIGN (session #98, 2026-07-12) — the lockstep DFA advance: machinery survey + R-a resolution + increment decomposition

**Decision context.** RE-PROFILE #3 falsified the standalone `.5.d.3` premise (memo node-clone = 1.27%); the director chose **option A** — advance to `.5.d.4` (the lockstep DFA advance + arena), the ONLY lever that moves the dominant 54.6% construction malloc, folding `.5.d.3` (memo sharing) and `.5.d.2` (lazy winner-only materialization) in as composing pieces. Docs-first design (NO code); the code increment is gated on the R-a agreement below (a pre-registered pause-and-agree point).

**Machinery survey (tool-grounded — `ast_based_generator.rs` `generate_or_logic`, verified line refs against the main/`.5.c.2` baseline).** The multi-branch tournament (`generate_or_logic`, `:3495`) is a SERIAL try-each:
- `parse_start = parser.position` (`:4024`); `best_end = parse_start`, `best_content/best_raw_content = None`, `best_priority = i64::MIN`, `best_branch_index/best_branch = 0`, `nonassoc_tie = false` (`:4024`–`:4031`).
- For each branch `idx` (via a `match idx { … }` over `branch_attempt_arms`, each optionally wrapped in the `.5.c.2` first-set prune guard `:3976`): `parser.position = parse_start` (`:3710`), `try_parse` the branch body (`:3711`), and on success capture `candidate_end = parser.position` (`:3722`), `raw_content` (`:3725`), run the return-annotation transform BEFORE rollback (`:3735`, BRANCH-BROADCAST-FIX.3 for `$text`), `parser.position = parse_start` (`:3739`), evaluate branch `@predicate`s (`:3742`), extract the C3-B `SemanticRuntimeDelta` (`:3927`), then `should_take` (`:3865`–`:3913`) decides via longest-match (`candidate_end > best_end`) with priority/`@associativity` tie-break (`right` → later branch wins; `nonassoc` equal-priority tie → `nonassoc_tie=true`). If `should_take`: update `best_end/best_content/best_branch_index/best_branch/best_semantic_delta`.
- After all branches: if `nonassoc_tie` → `Err(Backtrack)` (`:4069`); else `parser.position = best_end` (`:4074`), emit the `🏁 selected branch` trace, apply the winner's delta once (`apply_delta`, `:4098`), apply the winner's branch-start inline effects (`:4006`), return the winner's content. Cost = O(Σ branch-cost) with a full rollback per branch — the serial-try waste `.5.d.4` eliminates.

**R-a — furthest_position preservation (RESOLVED; the make-or-break correctness risk).** TOOL-VERIFIED FACT: `furthest_position` is written at EXACTLY ONE site — rule-method entry (`ast_based_generator.rs:3288` `if self.position > self.furthest_position { self.furthest_position = self.position }`; ALL 274 sites in `generated/regex_parser.rs` are that identical pattern; `match_string`/terminal matching NEVER write it; the interpreter mirrors it at `parse_harness_interpreter.rs:851`). It is monotone and never rolled back on speculation. Consequences for the lockstep advance:
- A **terminal branch** (regex `atom` alternative like `\d`, `\w`, a class shorthand, a literal) enters NO nested rule method, so its ONLY furthest contribution is the Or-rule's own entry = `parse_start` — already recorded before the tournament. A multi-char terminal that matches k chars then mismatches does NOT bump furthest beyond `parse_start` (match_string never writes it). ⇒ terminal branches are **furthest-neutral by construction** — the exact `.5.c.2` soundness argument (`:3983` "pruning is furthest_position-neutral, the rule entry already recorded parse_start").
- A **group-family branch** (`group`/`lookaround`/… all share the `(` introducer, discriminated by a deterministic longer prefix `(?:`/`(?P<`/`(?=`/`(?!`/`(`) : at a given position at most one prefix matches; the lockstep discriminates it and RECURSES into that branch via normal recursive-descent (which enters the nested rules and bumps furthest EXACTLY as today), while the non-matching group branches fail SHALLOW at the prefix (furthest ≤ prefix mismatch) — the same shallow reach the serial try gives them. ⇒ furthest preserved in both the recursive part (unchanged) and the discrimination part (shallow, ≤ serial).
- **Backstop:** `parse_harness_equivalence_gate` asserts byte-level `furthest_position` equality (interp[serial] vs generated[lockstep], ALL grammars, seeds 0/7/42) + `parse_harness_combinator_gate` covers the `a|ab` longest-match discrimination — so any lost furthest bump FAILS the gate. R-a is furthest-neutral by the verified single-write invariant AND oracle-checked.

**R-a AGREEMENT ASK (pre-registered pause-and-agree, [[feedback_stick_to_agreed_plan_no_silent_drift]]).** The R-a resolution above (furthest-neutral by the single rule-entry write + deterministic prefix discrimination + the equivalence-gate furthest backstop) is the proposed solution for the pre-registered R-a risk. Surfaced for the director's agreement BEFORE the first `.5.d.4` code increment. **→ ✅ AGREED (director, session #99, 2026-07-12, AskUserQuestion): "Greenlight — start increment (i) now."**

**Capability gate (parser-agnostic, CAPABILITY not grammar-name — [[feedback_features_parser_agnostic_enable_all_parsers]]).** The lockstep advance applies to a choice site whose branches are DFA-discriminable: each branch is (a) a pure terminal/regular sequence, or (b) a terminal-prefix-then-recurse form where the shared introducer is discriminated deterministically and the recursion runs as normal recursive-descent. Excluded (stay serial): branches with a branch-phase `@predicate` whose liveness depends on the store (F5c precision 3 — they stay live until the predicate resolves), and branches whose furthest contribution is NOT ≤ their discrimination point (non-prefix-disjoint recursive branches). The gate is a codegen capability check on the branch set, emitted per choice site; a site that fails the gate keeps today's byte-identical serial tournament.

**R-d (arena × `'input` lifetime) — DESIGN NEXT (not yet resolved).** The winner's AST is built once into an arena freed en masse; the memo returns a shared handle (`.5.d.3` folded in). The open question is the ownership/lifetime shape: `ParseNode<'input>` borrows the input; an arena/`Rc` handle must coexist with `'input`. Candidate: an arena keyed to the parse (dropped with the parser), nodes as arena indices or `&'arena`-borrowed, with `'arena: 'input` or a two-lifetime `ParseNode<'input, 'arena>`. This is the next design increment (R-d is its own pre-registered pause-and-agree point).

**Increment decomposition (each: design → decisive drift-controlled `regex_perf_probe` before→after → full ⛔ HARD-CONSTRAINT oracle battery → land iff faster + byte-identical).** (i) the terminal-only lockstep advance for the `atom` choice (the biggest, simplest DFA-discriminable site; folds in lazy winner-only materialization since a lockstep advance cannot transform per-branch) — measures the win from eliminating serial try + rollback on the hottest site; (ii) extend to group-family prefix discrimination; (iii) the arena + memo sharing (R-d) — the piece that attacks the 54.6% construction malloc at the root; (iv) generalize the capability gate across choice sites. Sequenced smallest-risk-first; each independently measurable so a null (like `.5.d.2`/`.5.d.3`) is caught before the next. The three F5c precisions (longest-match winner, lazy AST/effects, store-gated liveness) are preserved by every increment; the oracle is the `parse_harness_*` byte-identical differential + the full battery.

### `.5.d.4` R-d DESIGN (session #99, 2026-07-12) — arena × `'input` lifetime: ownership-model survey + recommendation + the pre-registered pause-and-agree

**Why now (plan-adherent, docs-only).** MEMORY next_action sequenced this: agree R-a → **design R-d** → first code increment. R-d is the second pre-registered pause-and-agree point (the tree's UNFORESEEN-ISSUE PROTOCOL). Designing it now — like `-0019` designed R-a — lets both pause points be surfaced for a single combined director agreement, so the whole `.5.d.4` road is greenlit as a unit before any code. NO code change here (design only).

**The two deep-clone sites R-d must eliminate (TOOL-VERIFIED, `ast_based_generator.rs` `memoized_call`).** (1) clone-on-INSERT `:7185` — `MemoEntry { result: Some(node.clone()), raw_semantic_content: raw_semantic_content.clone(), … }` deep-clones the whole `ParseNode<'input>` subtree into the memo on every success; (2) clone-on-HIT `:7130` — `return Ok((node.clone(), entry.raw_semantic_content.clone()))` deep-clones it back OUT on every cache hit. `ParseNode::clone` recurses through `ParseContent` (`mod.rs:715`): `Sequence(Vec<ParseNode>)` / `Alternative(Box<ParseNode>)` / `Quantified(Vec<ParseNode>)` deep-copy their children; `Json(serde_json::Value)` clones the map; only `Terminal(&'input str)` is a cheap pointer copy. RE-PROFILE #3 measured the memo-clone FRACTION at 1.27% — so R-d's real prize is NOT the memo clone per se but the **54.6% broad construction malloc** the arena attacks (bulk-alloc all nodes + mass-free losers), with memo sharing (`.5.d.3`) folded in as the cheap by-product.

**The `'input` borrow (the constraint that makes this non-trivial).** `ParseContent::Terminal(&'input str)` means the parse tree BORROWS the input, which outlives the parse. Any arena/handle scheme MUST preserve that borrow — this is the "coexist with `'input`" crux R-d flags.

**Blast radius (TOOL-VERIFIED) — and why it is SMALLER than it looks.** `ParseNode`/`ParseContent` are referenced across **~22 source files** (`grep -rln`). BUT the **public/crate boundary returns OWNED data, never a borrowed tree**: `parser_registry::parse_sample_ast_json*` → `serde_json::Value`; `embedding_api` → `Result<String,…>` (serialized). The tree is walked to owned JSON at the boundary via `ParseContent::to_json_value` (`mod.rs:731`) and the arena/tree is dropped. ⇒ `ParseNode<'input>` NEVER escapes borrowed to a consumer, so an arena (or even a second lifetime) is confined to the **internal parse machinery + the one boundary serialize walk** — the public API is UNCHANGED by construction. This de-risks the whole change. (Also TOOL-VERIFIED: no arena crate — `bumpalo`/`typed-arena`/`id-arena`/`slotmap` — is in `Cargo.toml` today.)

**Three candidate ownership models (each weighed against: moves the 54.6%? · `'input` coexistence · blast radius · new dep · folds `.5.d.3`?).**

- **(A) `Rc<ParseNode<'input>>` shared handles — single lifetime, ref-counted sharing.** Memo stores `Rc<ParseNode<'input>>`: insert = one `Rc::new`, hit = `Rc::clone` (pointer + refcount bump, ZERO deep-clone). Optionally children become `Rc` for true subtree sharing. **Moves 54.6%? NO** — still one heap alloc PER node (`Rc::new`), no bulk-alloc, no mass-free; it only removes the memo re-clone. **`'input`:** trivial (single lifetime preserved). **Blast radius:** small. **New dep:** none. **Folds `.5.d.3`:** yes (that IS `.5.d.3`). **Verdict:** a clean low-risk SHARING primitive but INSUFFICIENT ALONE for the dominant cost — predicted ~1% (exactly the RE-PROFILE #3 finding for standalone `.5.d.3`). Value = a composing piece, not the lever.

- **(B) Bump arena + `&'arena` borrows — two lifetimes `ParseNode<'input, 'arena>`, `'arena: 'input`.** A `bumpalo`-style arena tied to the parse; nodes are `&'arena ParseNode<'input, 'arena>`; the winner is walked to owned JSON at the boundary, then the arena is dropped (losers freed EN MASSE — one free, not K). **Moves 54.6%? YES** — bulk alloc + mass-free, the actual lever. **`'input`:** needs the `'arena: 'input` outlives-bound + a self-referential `&'arena ParseNode<'input,'arena>` (the classic arena-of-self pattern). **Blast radius:** TWO lifetimes propagate across the internal machinery (~22 files' internal uses) — but NOT the public API (owned boundary). **New dep:** `bumpalo`. **Folds `.5.d.3`:** yes (memo stores `&'arena ParseNode`, hit returns the shared borrow).

- **(C) Plain-`Vec` arena + `NodeId(u32)` indices — single `'input` lifetime, indices not borrows. ★ RECOMMENDED.** All nodes live in one `Vec<ParseNode<'input>>` arena; children are referenced by a `NodeId(u32)` index (`Sequence(Vec<NodeId>)`, `Alternative(NodeId)`, `Quantified(Vec<NodeId>)`) instead of `Box`/`Vec<ParseNode>`. Memo stores a `NodeId` + `end_pos`; a hit returns the `NodeId` (a `Copy` — ZERO clone). The arena is one growable Vec (bulk alloc via doubling), dropped at parse end (mass free); contiguous layout is cache-friendly (the simdjson tape idea). **Moves 54.6%? YES** — same bulk-alloc + mass-free as B. **`'input`:** stays a SINGLE lifetime — indices are `u32`, so no second lifetime and no self-referential borrow; the arena `Vec<ParseNode<'input>>` simply holds the same `'input` borrow the tree always had. **Blast radius:** every tree-WALKER (`to_json_value` `mod.rs:731`, the return-transform `ast_return_transform.rs`, the interpreter `parse_harness_interpreter.rs:*`, the AST-shape dumper) must thread the arena `&[ParseNode]` to resolve `NodeId` — a mechanical, contained refactor (no lifetime virality). **New dep:** NONE. **Folds `.5.d.3`:** yes (memo stores a `NodeId`, hit is a Copy).

**RECOMMENDATION — Candidate C.** It delivers the same bulk-allocation + mass-free that moves the dominant 54.6% construction malloc as B, AND folds in `.5.d.3` memo sharing (memo → `NodeId`, hit is a Copy), while keeping the SINGLE `'input` lifetime — avoiding B's viral two-lifetime `ParseNode<'input,'arena>` propagation and adding NO new dependency. The price is threading the arena slice through the handful of tree-walkers to resolve `NodeId`, which is mechanical and confined (vs a lifetime that infects every internal `ParseNode` signature). The public boundary is unchanged (walk to owned `serde_json::Value`, drop arena). Byte-identical by construction: the tree is structurally identical, merely referenced by index instead of `Box`/`Vec` — the boundary JSON is the same bytes, proven by the `parse_harness_*` differential (verdict + AST + `furthest_position`, all grammars, seeds 0/7/42) + the PCRE2 per-case diff + the all-parsers byte-identical regen. (If C's walker-threading proves unexpectedly heavy in practice, B is the fallback — same performance envelope, lifetime cost instead of index-threading cost; A is retained only as the `.5.d.3` sharing sub-step if we ever want memo sharing WITHOUT the arena.)

**Sequencing (important — R-d is increment (iii), not (i)).** The FIRST code increment (i) — the terminal-only lockstep advance for `atom` — does NOT need the arena; it eliminates serial try + rollback and folds in lazy materialization, measurable on its own. The arena (R-d) lands at increment (iii), the piece that attacks the 54.6% at the root. So R-d is agreed **in principle NOW** (greenlight the arena DIRECTION — candidate C — for the road), with the concrete arena code deferred to increment (iii) after (i)/(ii) measure the lockstep win. Both R-a and R-d are surfaced before ANY code precisely so the director greenlights the whole `.5.d.4` shape as a unit (the pre-registered pause-and-agree discipline).

**R-d AGREEMENT ASK (pre-registered pause-and-agree, [[feedback_stick_to_agreed_plan_no_silent_drift]]).** Recommend **candidate C** (plain-`Vec` arena + `NodeId(u32)` indices, single `'input` lifetime, no new dep) as the arena ownership model for increment (iii); B (two-lifetime `&'arena`) is the fallback, A (`Rc`-only) is insufficient alone. Surfaced for the director's agreement (direction only; the arena code is increment (iii), after increment (i)) alongside the R-a resolution, BEFORE the first `.5.d.4` code increment. **→ ✅ AGREED: candidate C (director, session #99, 2026-07-12, AskUserQuestion). B is the fallback if C's index-threading proves heavy; the arena code lands at increment (iii).**

### `.5.d.4.i` — CODE INCREMENT (i): terminal-only lockstep advance for the `atom` choice (session #99, 2026-07-12) — director-AGREED, IN PROGRESS

**Scope (the smallest, safest first increment).** Replace the SERIAL try-each-branch tournament with a lockstep forward scan ONLY for the `atom` choice site (regex.ebnf:365, the hottest DFA-discriminable site) and ONLY its terminal-family branches; folds in lazy winner-only materialization (a lockstep advance cannot transform per-branch). Every other choice site + any non-terminal `atom` branch keeps today's byte-identical serial tournament (the capability gate). Parser-agnostic codegen (`generate_or_logic`), gated on CAPABILITY not grammar-name. NO arena yet (that is increment (iii), R-d candidate C) — increment (i) measures the win from eliminating serial-try + rollback alone.

**⚠️ PRE-REGISTERED risk to watch (the `.5.d.2`/`.5.d.3` pattern).** `.5.c.2` first-set dispatch already prunes most losing branches before they are tried, so the serial-try + rollback overhead increment (i) removes may already be small ⇒ increment (i) alone COULD be a null (like `.5.d.2`/`.5.d.3`), with the real 54.6%-construction-malloc win coming at increment (iii) (the arena) that this restructuring enables. Per the decomposition this is measured EXPLICITLY: if increment (i) is byte-identical-but-neutral, it does NOT land standalone (HARD CONSTRAINT) — the lockstep restructuring is then re-introduced combined with the arena at (iii), exactly as lazy materialization (`.5.d.2`) is re-introduced here. A null is DATA, caught before (iii). If a genuinely unforeseen issue surfaces mid-implementation → PAUSE + analyze + agree (UNFORESEEN-ISSUE PROTOCOL).

#### ROOT-CAUSE ANALYSIS (session #99, before building — the `.5.d.3` precedent: predict the marginal gain from the profile FIRST)

Increment (i)'s acceptance checklist begins with REPRODUCE + ROOT CAUSE the cost it removes. Doing that against the committed RE-PROFILE #3 profile (33,671 leaf samples on the current on-disk eager `.5.c.2` baseline) BEFORE building — exactly the `.5.d.3` method ("profile the target cost specifically before the invasive change"):

- A TERMINAL-ONLY lockstep advance removes the SERIAL try-each per-branch **backtrack** cost: `rollback_to_named` (469 samples) + `extract_delta_since` (151) = **620 samples = 1.84% of self-time**. `directives_for_rule` (503 = 1.49%) is NOT removed (the lockstep still consults per-rule directives). Folding in lazy winner-only materialization adds **≈0** — `.5.d.2` already MEASURED lazy-vs-eager as a null (because `.5.c.2` first-set dispatch prunes the losing branches before they are tried/transformed).
- ⇒ **increment (i) TERMINAL-ONLY standalone ceiling ≈ 1.8%** — below the decisive-separation bar every landed lever cleared, and below the ~3% inter-round variance at the ~76µs baseline. **PREDICTED NULL.**
- This is the **THIRD instance of the same pattern** (`.5.d.2`, `.5.d.3`, now `.5.d.4.i`), same root: `.5.c.2` already removed the losing-branch cost, so the residual dominant cost is the **54.6% construction malloc** that ONLY the arena (increment (iii), R-d candidate C) moves. A terminal-only lockstep WITHOUT the arena is a micro-opt on the ~2% backtrack bucket; the winner's node construction (the 54.6%) is untouched until the arena.
- **Increment (i) has no standalone LANDABLE value** either: the HARD CONSTRAINT is *land iff faster*, so a byte-identical-but-neutral increment (i) cannot land as a speed lever (the `.5.d.2` precedent — it was reverted, and "returns combined with the arena"). So building (i) standalone = a full fat-LTO cycle to confirm a predicted null, then the combined (i)+(iii) arena work happens anyway at (iii).

**⛔ DECISION SURFACED (not taken unilaterally — this DEVIATES from the agreed "measure each increment independently" decomposition, so [[feedback_stick_to_agreed_plan_no_silent_drift]]: flag loudly + agree BEFORE acting).** The `.5.d.3` precedent (predict from the profile, don't burn the build) says skip the standalone (i) measurement. Options put to the director (session #99): (A, recommended) **MERGE (i)+(iii)** — build the lockstep advance + arena (candidate C) as ONE landable change for the `atom` site, developed in LOCAL correctness stages (lockstep-first byte-identical via the cheap debug-build oracles, then the arena byte-identical) with the EXPENSIVE fat-LTO speed measurement run ONCE at the end on the change that actually wins — gets the small-step isolation for CORRECTNESS while avoiding a wasted fat-LTO cycle on a predicted-null standalone (i); (B) build (i) standalone first anyway for the clean empirical null data point (a full fat-LTO cycle), then (iii); (C) something else / discuss. The agreed DESTINATION (the lockstep road + arena → PCRE2 parity) is UNCHANGED — this is a WITHIN-road sequencing refinement.

#### ✅ SEQUENCING AGREED (director, session #99, AskUserQuestion): MERGE (i)+(iii)

Director chose **(A) MERGE (i)+(iii)** — build the lockstep advance + arena (candidate C) as ONE landable change for the `atom` site, developed in LOCAL correctness stages (lockstep byte-identical via cheap debug oracles → arena byte-identical), with the EXPENSIVE fat-LTO speed measurement run ONCE at the end. This WITHIN-road re-sequencing (vs the agreed "measure each independently" decomposition) is now the plan; the DESTINATION (lockstep + arena → PCRE2 parity) is unchanged.

#### ✅ STEP 0 GATE RESULT (session #99) — the arena is JUSTIFIED (arena-movable ≈ 44% of allocation ≈ 24% of total self-time)

**Method.** Built the legacy `regex_perf_probe` (`parse_full_regex()` — the same path RE-PROFILE #3 used, exercising the return-annotation serde_json transforms; the typed probe needs a `--enable-parser-hooks` regen the on-disk parser lacks), sampled 30 s @1 ms (25,278 thread samples / 19,643 in `time_one_parse`), and attributed **every allocation leaf** (`malloc`/`free`/`memmove`/`memset`/`memcmp`/`bzero`) to its NEAREST call-graph ancestor (arena-movable PARSE vs irreducible SERDE vs SEM vs HASH). Raw: scratchpad `sample_arena_ceiling.txt` + `profile_arena_ceiling.sh`.

**Result (allocation attributed by nearest ancestor; ratios robust):**

| Bucket | Alloc share | Arena-movable (candidate C)? |
|---|---|---|
| **PARSE** — `ParseNode`/`ParseContent`/`Vec` construction+drop | **43.8%** | ✅ YES — the arena's target |
| SERDE — serde_json `Value`/`BTreeMap`/`to_json_value` output | 25.9% | ❌ no (the return-annotation JSON output; irreducible by a ParseNode arena) |
| SEM — `rollback_to_named`/`extract_delta_since`/`directives_for_rule` | 20.5% | ❌ no (semantic runtime) |
| OTHER / PREDSPLIT (`split_semantic_top_level` 2.6%) / HASH | ~10% | ❌ no |

**DECISION (gate PASSED — the arena surgery is JUSTIFIED, no re-surface needed; director already agreed to build the arena).** Allocation ≈ 54.6% of total self-time (RE-PROFILE #3), and **43.8% of it is arena-movable `ParseNode`/`Vec` construction ≈ ~24% of TOTAL self-time** — decisively above the ≳25%-of-allocation gate (43.8% ≫ 25%). Candidate C's arena is the single biggest remaining lever. **Serde_json output (26%) + semantic-runtime (20%) are NOT arena-movable** — so the arena alone will NOT reach PCRE2 parity; it is the biggest single next step, with a **serde_json-construction lever** (reduce the return-annotation `Value` building — 26%) and the **semantic-runtime bucket** (20%, partly the increment-(i) rollback/delta) as the two next-largest future levers surfaced by this profile. Proceed to the arena implementation (candidate C).

#### ⚠️ STEP 0 GATE (before the surgery) — profile the arena's REAL ceiling (the serde_json caveat)

A new caveat surfaced while root-causing: the 54.6% "construction malloc" bucket (RE-PROFILE #3) is *ALL* parse-structure allocation — "winner AST + **serde_json Values** + Vecs + Strings." **Candidate C's arena allocates `ParseNode`s, NOT the output `serde_json::Value`s** — `to_json_value` still builds the winner's `Value` tree at the boundary (the output, irreducible by an arena). So candidate C's REAL ceiling is the *transient* `ParseNode`/`Vec`/`Box` alloc fraction, NOT the full 54.6%. RE-PROFILE #3's own sub-splits hint the serde_json share is large (biggest clone = `Value::Object` 574 via `to_json_value`; drop bucket = `drop_in_place<serde_json::Value>` 491 + BTreeMap 528 vs ParseNode/ParseContent drops). Before a 13-file / 146-child-walk-site / high-risk arena surgery (`grep` blast radius, session #99), profile the malloc/free/drop bucket BY CALLER to split arena-movable (transient `ParseNode`/`Vec`) from irreducible (serde_json output). Decision rule: substantial movable fraction (say ≳25%) → build the arena (the surgery is justified); serde_json-output-dominated → surface + weigh a serde_json-construction lever instead. This is the `.5.d.3` discipline (profile the target cost before the invasive reshape) applied to the biggest reshape of the phase. Tool: `regex_typed_perf_probe --samples N` under macOS `sample`, decompose the malloc/free/drop call-tree.

#### ARENA SURGERY PLAN (candidate C — the focused next-session implementation blueprint)

The STEP 0 gate passed ⇒ implement candidate C (plain-`Vec` arena + `NodeId(u32)` indices). Concrete plumbing (do it as a FOCUSED, careful effort — this is the phase's riskiest byte-identity change; the ⛔ HARD CONSTRAINT is *land iff faster + byte-identical*):

- **Types (`mod.rs`).** Add `struct NodeId(u32)` (Copy). Change `ParseContent<'input>` children from owned to indices: `Sequence(Vec<NodeId>)`, `Alternative(NodeId)`, `Quantified(Vec<NodeId>, &'static str)` (Terminal/TransformedTerminal/Json stay — they hold no child `ParseNode`). Add an arena `Vec<ParseNode<'input>>` (the node store). `ParseNode` keeps `{rule_name, content, span}`.
- **Arena location.** A field on the generated parser struct (`arena: Vec<ParseNode<'input>>`), threaded as `&mut` for construction and `&` for walking. Alternative: a `ParseForest` wrapper returned alongside the root `NodeId`. Prefer the parser-struct field (fewer signature changes; the arena drops with the parser = mass-free).
- **Codegen (`ast_based_generator.rs`, 8 `ParseNode {` construction sites).** Each construction pushes to `self.arena` and yields a `NodeId`; rule methods return `NodeId` (or `(NodeId, raw)`), not owned `ParseNode`. `try_parse`/memo store `NodeId` (memo hit returns a `Copy` index — folds `.5.d.3`). The `generate_or_logic` tournament composes the lockstep advance here (folds `.5.d.2` lazy materialization — winner-only).
- **Walkers (thread the arena `&[ParseNode]` to resolve `NodeId`).** `to_json_value` (`mod.rs:731`), `ast_return_transform.rs`, `parse_harness_interpreter.rs` (interpreter must mirror the arena for byte-identity), `ast_shape_contract.rs`, the AST-dump path, `semantic_runtime.rs` content walks, `grammar_wellformedness.rs`, `unified_*_ast.rs`, the bins. ~146 `ParseContent::{Sequence,Alternative,Quantified}` match sites (`grep`), most mechanical (add `arena` param, `arena[id.0 as usize]`).
- **Public boundary UNCHANGED** — `parse_sample_ast_json*`/`embedding_api` walk the arena to owned `serde_json::Value` at the boundary, then drop the arena. Consumers see no change.
- **Byte-identity strategy (cheap DEBUG oracles, staged):** after the type change compiles, run `parse_harness_equivalence_gate` (interp↔generated, all grammars, seeds 0/7/42 — the interpreter MUST be migrated too), `parse_harness_combinator_gate` (27/27 + `a|ab`), `parse_harness_semantic_gate` (36/36) — all DEBUG builds, no fat-LTO. The typed AST is structurally identical (index-addressed), so boundary JSON is the same bytes. Regen all 11 parsers (byte-identical logic). THEN one fat-LTO drift-controlled `regex_perf_probe` measurement + PCRE2 per-case diff + cert + duality + ast-shape + clippy.
- **Migration order (smallest-broken-window):** (1) types + arena field + `NodeId` in `mod.rs`; (2) the interpreter (`parse_harness_interpreter.rs`) — it constructs `ParseContent` directly, so migrate it in lockstep; (3) codegen construction + regen; (4) the walkers one file at a time until it compiles; (5) oracles. Expect a multi-file broken window until (4) completes — do it in one focused pass, commit only at compiling+byte-identical.

#### ✅ ARENA MODEL DECISION (session #100, 2026-07-12) — candidate C CONFIRMED (C-vs-B re-weighed pre-code, tool-backed; the Drop-correctness decider)

**Context.** Before writing a line of the phase's riskiest byte-identity change, a tool-backed scoping pass re-weighed the C-vs-B choice the director made in session #99. Two findings changed the picture; the director then delegated the call ("you are the expert coder — pick the SOTA, signoff, most durable, clean, rock-solid approach and roll"). Decision: **candidate C, on firmer Drop-aware footing.**

**Finding 1 — the type change is SINGLE-SITE (shrinks the surgery).** TOOL-VERIFIED the generated parsers `use crate::ast_pipeline::{… ParseContent, ParseNode, MemoEntry, …}` (`generated/regex_parser.rs:3`) — they do NOT define their own copy. So `ParseContent`/`ParseNode`/`MemoEntry` live in exactly ONE place (`mod.rs`); the blueprint's "two copies in lockstep" worry is void. `ast_code_generator.rs` (its inert `quote!` template with a simpler `ParseContent`) is legacy/uninvoked (no `AstCodeGenerator::new` outside its own file) — leave it alone.

**Finding 2 — the crux the blueprint mislabeled "mechanical."** `ast_return_transform.rs` (the return-annotation codegen — historically the most byte-identity-fragile area: PGEN-RGX-0075/0077, the `<invalid_sequence_access>` sentinels) EMITS runtime code that treats `ParseContent` children as OWNED, MOVABLE values: `for node in nodes { array_elements.push(node) }` (moves children out of `Sequence`/`Quantified`), `elements[N].content.clone()` (owned index), `while let Alternative(node) = current { current = node.content }` (peel by move), and mid-transform `ParseNode { rule_name, content: …, span }` CONSTRUCTION (spread/flatten-spread wrappers). ~15 such emitted sites (`grep` verified: lines 174/176/191/223-233/284-339/424-436/501/559-562/615-627/671/851). Under C (`Vec<NodeId>` children on a `self.arena`) these are NOT a mechanical `arena[id]` swap — a fresh wrapper must be `arena.push`'d (`&mut`) while the same loop reads `arena[child]` (`&`) — a borrow interleave to hand-resolve byte-identically, in the fragile codegen.

**The C-vs-B re-weigh + the decider (Drop-correctness).** `ParseContent` carries `String` (`TransformedTerminal`) and `serde_json::Value` (`Json`) — heap-owning `Drop` types. Any arena MUST run their destructors or LEAK on every parse:
- **C (`Vec<ParseNode>` + `NodeId`)** — Drop-correct FOR FREE (the `Vec` drops every element). Single `'input` lifetime. No dep. Memo stores a `NodeId` (Copy) ⇒ folds `.5.d.3` sharing. ✅
- **B / plain `bumpalo`** — `Bump` does NOT run `Drop` ⇒ leaks every `String`/`Value` in the tree, unbounded. ✗ DISQUALIFIED (a hazard the session-#99 surface under-weighted; `bumpalo` is transitively in `Cargo.lock:98` but is NOT a direct pgen dep).
- **B / `typed-arena` (the Drop-correct ref-arena, rustc `TypedArena` shape)** — works, but adds a dep AND the viral `'arena` second lifetime.

**Durability decider (the director's stated criterion).** `ParseContent`/`ParseNode` is a CORE type touched by 13 files. A viral `'arena` lifetime (`ParseNode<'input,'arena>`) is a PERMANENT signature tax across all of them, complicating every future change. **rust-analyzer** — a large, long-lived, signoff-grade codebase — deliberately chose INDEX arenas (`la_arena::Idx`) over reference arenas for its syntax trees for exactly this reason (no lifetime virality, simpler signatures, serializable). Strong SOTA precedent that the single-lifetime index arena is the cleaner, more durable choice for a large tree-heavy codebase. The public boundary is already owned (`serde_json::Value`), so C's arena is a purely internal detail either way.

**⇒ DECISION (SUPERSEDED — see the REVISION below): candidate C** — Drop-correct by construction, single `'input` lifetime (no viral tax), no naive-bumpalo leak, `la_arena`-grade precedent, folds `.5.d.3` memo sharing. The transform crux is handled with a disciplined **read-then-push** borrow pattern. This conclusion was REVERSED within the same session by a second tool-backed finding (below); the Findings 1/2 + the Drop-correctness analysis above remain valid and inform candidate B too.

#### 🔁 ARENA MODEL DECISION — REVISED to candidate B (session #100, 2026-07-12, director-confirmed) — the derived-`Serialize` oracle is the decider

**The finding that reversed C→B (tool-backed).** The authoritative typed-AST — the exact bytes EVERY differential gate compares (`parse_harness_equivalence` asserts `interp.ast_json == oracle_value`) and `parse_sample_ast_json` returns — is **`serde_json::to_value(&ParseNode)` via `#[derive(Serialize)]`**, at THREE boundary sites: `parser_registry.rs:1083` (`parse_node_to_json`), `parse_harness_interpreter.rs:416`, `parse_harness.rs:457`. NOT `to_json_value` (which is a SEPARATE return-transform-internal path). Consequence:
- **Candidate C (`Sequence(Vec<NodeId>)`) BREAKS the oracle** — the derive would emit BARE INDICES (`{"Sequence":[0,5,12]}`) instead of the nested subtree, and `serde::Serialize::serialize(&self, S)` has NO channel to receive the arena to resolve them. C would force a hand-written, byte-PERFECT reimplementation of serde's externally-tagged derive (enum tagging, `Range`→`{start,end}`, `Quantified` tuple-as-array) — i.e. reproducing THE ORACLE ITSELF by hand. The single most byte-identity-dangerous change possible; directly threatens the #1 immovable correctness floor.
- **Candidate B (`&'arena` references) PRESERVES the oracle FOR FREE** — serde serializes `&T`/`&[T]` byte-identically to `T`, so `#[derive(Serialize)]` on `ParseNode<'input,'arena>` with `Alternative(&'arena ParseNode)`/`Sequence(&'arena [ParseNode])` produces the SAME bytes as today's `Box`/`Vec`. The fragile return-transform code barely changes (children stay dereferenceable; `for node in nodes` still works; construction is `arena.alloc(...)` with no `&mut`/`&` borrow interleave since the arena hands out `&'arena` via interior mutability).

**Drop-correctness (resolved for B).** `ParseContent` carries `String`+`serde_json::Value` (heap `Drop`). Plain `bumpalo` (the only vendored arena, `Cargo.lock:98`) does NOT run `Drop` ⇒ would leak every parse ✗. **`typed-arena`** (small, standard, zero-transitive-deps, the rustc `TypedArena` shape) IS Drop-correct (runs destructors on arena drop) AND hands out `&'arena mut T` from `&self` (build children-then-parents; parents reference children). So B = `typed_arena::Arena<ParseNode<'input,'arena>>`, created at the boundary, passed as `&'arena Arena` to the parser (NOT owned by it ⇒ no self-referential struct), dropped after the boundary serialize walk = mass-free. Memo stores `Option<&'arena ParseNode>` (Copy) ⇒ folds `.5.d.3` sharing for free.

**Cost of B (accepted):** a viral `'arena` second lifetime on `ParseNode`/`ParseContent`/`MemoEntry` and every internal signature touching them — mechanical, COMPILER-CHECKED (a slip is a compile error, never a silent byte diff), and INTERNAL-ONLY (the public boundary returns owned `serde_json::Value`, so `'arena` never leaks to consumers) — plus one new direct dep (`typed-arena`).

**⇒ DECISION: candidate B via `typed-arena`** (director-confirmed, session #100, AskUserQuestion: "B via typed-arena dep (recommended)"). Rationale honors the director's stated criteria (SOTA/signoff/durable/clean/ROCK-SOLID) with correctness-floor primacy: B keeps the byte-identity oracle intact by construction (the dominant consideration), vs C reproducing the oracle by hand. The viral lifetime is the price, paid in compiler-checked mechanical churn, not byte-identity risk. Byte-identity still gated CHEAPLY by the debug oracles before any fat-LTO.

**Revised migration order (candidate B):** (1) add `typed-arena` dep; (2) `mod.rs` — `ParseContent<'input,'arena>`/`ParseNode<'input,'arena>`/`MemoEntry<'input,'arena>` with `&'arena` children + the `'arena` lifetime; `to_json_value` needs NO arena param (children are refs); (3) interpreter (`parse_harness_interpreter.rs`) — build into an `Arena`, mirror the lifetime; (4) codegen (`ast_based_generator.rs`) — the parser struct gains `arena: &'arena Arena<ParseNode<'input,'arena>>`, construction sites `self.arena.alloc(ParseNode{...})`, rule methods return `&'arena ParseNode`, memo stores the ref; (5) thread `'arena` through the walkers until it compiles (mostly signature churn, bodies unchanged); (6) regen 11 parsers + debug oracles (equivalence/combinator/semantic) + fat-LTO measure. Boundary sites (`parse_node_to_json` etc.) create the `Arena` and pass it in. Land iff faster + byte-identical.

#### 🔨 IMPLEMENTATION PROGRESS (session #100, 2026-07-12) — runtime lib migrated (compiles); codegen + regen + oracles remain

**Design refinement CONFIRMED (strict improvement within candidate B): SINGLE lifetime, not viral two-lifetime.** The arena's nodes reborrow the input down to the ARENA's scope, so ONE lifetime parameter `'input` (now = the arena/parse scope, ≤ the true input) expresses both "borrows input" and "borrows arena" — the standard `typed_arena` cyclic-graph pattern. So `ParseNode<'input>` stays SINGLE-parameter across the codebase (the durability win that made C attractive, now WITH B's byte-identity safety). This collapsed the feared "viral lifetime across 250 sites" to ~19 localized fixes.

**DONE this session (IN-FLIGHT, UNCOMMITTED — ~77 lines, 4 files; the default-feature lib COMPILES clean):**
- `Cargo.toml`: `typed-arena = "2.0.2"` added (verified fetch, zero transitive deps, Drop-correct).
- `mod.rs`: `pub type NodeArena<'input> = typed_arena::Arena<ParseNode<'input>>`; `ParseContent` children → `&'input` refs (`Sequence(Vec<&'input ParseNode>)`, `Alternative(&'input ParseNode)`, `Quantified(Vec<&'input ParseNode>, _)`); `MemoEntry.result` → `Option<&'input ParseNode>` (the memo-sharing fold). `to_json_value` UNCHANGED (children auto-deref); `#[derive(Serialize/PartialEq)]` UNCHANGED (refs transparent) — the whole reason for B.

- `unified_return_ast.rs`: the return-AST walker's slice helpers take `&[&'input ParseNode]` (Sequence/Quantified now hold refs): `sequence_elements`/`sequence_child_rule`/`quantified_items`/`parse_generated_parenthesized_sequence`.

### `.5` RE-PROFILE #4 (session #103, 2026-07-12) — post-arena steering; the arena's effect CONFIRMED; a NEW dominant bucket surfaced: per-parse CONSTRUCTION of grammar-constant tables (11.9%)

**Method (the land→re-profile→steer discipline, RE-PROFILE #2/#3 precedent).** Rebuilt the arena `regex_perf_probe` from HEAD (`961b1781`, fat-LTO, sha256 `8b463969…` — differs from the stale on-disk owned baseline `b0fda7b2…`; also from the measurement session's `40def90f…` because this build embeds all 11 parsers instead of the reduced measurement set; same regex hot path). **Drift-control sanity PASSED:** geomean-of-mins ≈ **60.1µs** single-round (pinned arena ≈58.6µs; owned baseline ≈75µs — the arena baseline reproduces). Then macOS `sample` 30s @ 1ms in steady state: **25,189 thread samples / 24,956 in `time_one_parse`**. Raw: scratchpad `sample_reprofile4.txt` + `analyze_sample.py` (bucket attribution: allocation leaves → nearest named ancestor, the STEP-0 method).

**Result 1 — the arena did exactly what STEP 0 predicted.** Allocation-family leaves collapsed **54.6% → 17.7% of self-time** (4447/25189). Residual alloc attribution: PARSE 53.4% of alloc (arena chunk growth + `Vec<&ParseNode>` accumulators + memo inserts + Strings) / SEM 18.2% / SERDE 17.9% / STRING 5.5% / HASH 4.1%.

**Result 2 — the NEW #1 coherent bucket: `RegexParser::new` = 11.9% of the timed subtree (2962/24,956).** Mechanism (tool-named, generated `regex_parser.rs:640-3000`, emitted by the `ast_based_generator.rs` constructor template): every parse re-builds the GRAMMAR-CONSTANT compiled annotation tables — 30 `directives_by_rule.insert` into a **std::HashMap (SipHash — 1360 of the insert samples sit under `new()`)**, **92 `.to_string()` heap allocs**, `Vec`/directive-struct allocs — then `from_parts` converts std→`FxHashMap` (**every key hashed a SECOND time**), then the whole table is **dropped** at parse end (`drop_in_place<SemanticRuntimeDirective>` 0.85%). This is the session-#90 "construction floor" hypothesis that was CORRECTLY refuted at the 496µs baseline (then ~0.7%): the cost is FIXED per parse, so 8.5× faster parses raised its share ~8.5× → now the top bucket. The embedding boundary (`parse_sample_ast_json`, RGX per-pattern compile) constructs a parser per parse, so this is REAL consumer cost, not probe artifact.

**Result 3 — the rest of the flat profile (no other single bucket ≥10%):**
| Bucket | ≈ share | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `new()` construction (grammar-constant) | **11.9%** | the Result-2 mechanism; lever `.5.g` below |
| Rule-call machinery (parse_* closures) | ~12% distributed | `parse_literal_char` 2.28 + `parse_piece` 2.08 + `parse_atom` 3.27 + `parse_letter` 1.86 + `parse_literal` 1.03 + … — the `.5.e` GLL/lockstep target; NO single hotspot |
| SEM residual | ~8% | `directives_for_rule` 1.68 + `rollback_to_named` 1.30 + `extract_delta_since` 0.85 + directive drop 0.85 + SEM-attributed alloc 3.22 |
| SERDE output | ~6% | BTreeMap clone 1.04 + `drop<Value>` 1.04 + SERDE-attributed alloc 3.16 (the return-annotation JSON output — irreducible by parse-side levers; the STEP-0 "serde lever" if ever) |
| Vec/slice clones | ~4.5% | `Vec::clone` 1.75 + `to_vec` 1.70 + `ParseContent::clone` 1.02 — memo-hit + `raw_content` clones |
| SipHash total | 2.78% | mostly under `new()` (dies with `.5.g`); residual parse-time fact-emission inserts (`parse_zero_width` 207 + `parse_quantifier` 177 ≈ 1.5%) = a small `.5.a`-repeatable FxHash lever |
| Probe timing overhead | ~2.1% | `mach_absolute_time` — not parser cost |

**⇒ STEERING (surfaced to the director per [[feedback_stick_to_agreed_plan_no_silent_drift]] — the agreed order says `.5.e` GLL is next; this proposes a WITHIN-road insertion, the RE-PROFILE #3 precedent).** The profile-indicated cheapest-biggest lever is NOT the research-grade `.5.e` but a **construction cache** (new leaf `.5.g` proposed): build the grammar-constant `CompiledSemanticRuntimeAnnotations` ONCE per process (e.g. `std::sync::OnceLock`/`Arc` in the generated parser; `new()` clones the `Arc`), killing the per-parse std-HashMap build + double hash + 92 Strings + table drop. Parser-AGNOSTIC codegen/engine change; byte-identical BY CONSTRUCTION (same table VALUES; the maps are point-lookup-only — the `.5.a` proof); ceiling ≈ **8–11%** concentrated, LOW risk vs `.5.e`'s research-grade risk on a ~12% DISTRIBUTED bucket. Recommended sequence: `.5.g` (cheap, 1 session) → `.5.e` (the road's destination rung, unchanged). Verify-plan for `.5.g`: confirm no per-instance mutation of the compiled table post-construction (`set_fact_kinds` happens inside the build; `set_grammar_profile`/library dirs touch OTHER fields), then the standard battery + fat-LTO alternated measure, land iff faster + byte-identical.

See the `.5.g` IMPLEMENTATION + MEASURE section below.)  Goal: **CONSTRUCTION CACHE** — the generated parser's grammar-constant compiled annotation tables built once per process and shared (`OnceLock`; `new()` takes the `&'static` handle), eliminating the per-parse rebuild (11.9% bucket: SipHash std-HashMap inserts + std→Fx double-hash + 92 `to_string` + drop). Parser-agnostic codegen+engine; byte-identical by construction; ⛔ HARD CONSTRAINT applies (land iff faster + byte-identical).

### `.5.g` IMPLEMENTATION + MEASURE (session #103, 2026-07-12, `PGEN-RGX-0078-0033`) — CONSTRUCTION CACHE LANDED: −3.8%, byte-identical

**Sequencing agreement.** Director (AskUserQuestion, session #103): "`.5.g` first, then `.5.e`" — the RE-PROFILE #4 recommended within-road insertion; destination (PCRE2 parity via the lockstep road) unchanged.

**THE CHANGE (3 emission sites in `ast_based_generator.rs` — parser-AGNOSTIC codegen; all 11 parsers inherit it):**
- New emitted associated fn `shared_semantic_runtime_annotations()`: a `static OnceLock<CompiledSemanticRuntimeAnnotations>` holding the ENTIRE former per-parse construction block (`directives_by_rule`/`branch_directives_by_rule`/`fact_kinds` inserts + `from_parts` + `set_fact_kinds`), built once per process via `get_or_init`.
- The parser-struct field became `semantic_runtime_annotations: &'static CompiledSemanticRuntimeAnnotations`; `new()` takes the shared handle (`Self::shared_semantic_runtime_annotations()`) instead of rebuilding; the accessor returns the reference directly; `transaction_for_rule` takes the field without the extra `&`.
- SOUNDNESS PRECONDITION (tool-verified BEFORE building): `set_fact_kinds` is the compiled table's ONLY `&mut self` method and its only call site is INSIDE the construction block (`regex_parser.rs:982`, emitted by the same template) ⇒ the table is immutable after construction; all parse-time access is `&self` point lookups (`.5.a` proof); the one interior-mutability `Cell` in the file belongs to `SemanticRuntimeState` (per-parse mutable state, untouched) ⇒ `Sync` holds and the `static` compiles.

**MEASURE (decisive, drift-controlled — BOTH release fat-LTO, distinct sha256 `8b463969…` baseline / `d954f897…` cache, measured ALTERNATELY 5 rounds × 2000 samples, geomean-of-mins):** baseline `58638/58098/59502/59024/59762` ns vs cache `55743/56627/56884/57322/57194` ns → per-round ratios **`0.951/0.975/0.956/0.971/0.957`** = geomean **0.962 → −3.8%** (≈58.6µs→≈56.8µs), cache faster EVERY round, no sign flips; baseline reproduces the pinned `.5.d.4.i` ≈58.6µs. Honest note: below the 8–11% ceiling — the `new()` subtree also contains genuinely per-parse state init (memo pre-size, `SemanticRuntimeState::new`, `clone_predicate_defs`, struct init) that the cache correctly keeps; the removable part was the annotation-table build alone.
